      "id": "Cahaya Martebo (dalam bahasa Swedia Marteboljusen atau Marteboljuset) adalah \"cahaya hantu\" yang terlihat sejak awal 1900-an di sebuah jalan di Martebo, di pulau Gotland milik Swedia.       "id": "Mjöll adalah nama raksasa perempuan (Riesin) dalam mitologi Nordik. Karena dalam bahasa Nordik Kuno (Altnordischen) namanya berarti \"salju, salju baru atau salju bubuk\", ia kemungkinan tergolong bangsa raksasa embun beku (Reifriesen).",
      "en": "Mjöll is the name of a giantess (Riesin) in Norse mythology. Since in the Old Norse (Altnordischen) language her name means \"snow, new or powder snow\", she probably belongs to the race of frost giants (Reifriesen).",

        "mjoll-c02"

    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah Hversu Noregr byggdist di Flatey Book, Snær, personifikasi salju, mempunyai tiga putri: Fönn (gundukan salju), Drífa (salju turun), dan Mjöll (salju bubuk). Sturlaugs saga menyebut Raja Salju dari Finmark dan putrinya Mjöll yang terbang cepat di udara.",
      "en": "In the account Hversu Noregr byggdist in the Flatey Book, Snær, the personification of snow, has three daughters: Fönn (snowdrift), Drífa (snowfall) and Mjöll (powdered snow). Sturlaugs saga tells of King Snow of Finmark and his daughter Mjöll, who flies swiftly through the air.",

  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Mo-o (juga mo-ko) adalah hewan dongeng, naga laut besar dari Polinesia; di Hawaii, moʻo adalah roh kadal yang dapat berganti wujud.",
    "en": "The mo-o (also mo-ko) is a fabulous beast, the great sea dragon of Polynesia; in Hawaii the moʻo are shapeshifting lizard spirits.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "mo-o-c01",
      "mo-o-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mo-o (juga disebut mo-ko) adalah hewan dongeng (fabeldier), naga laut besar dari Polinesia (Polynesië). Dahulu di Polinesia berbagai fauna disebut Mo-O: buaya, aligator, belut, penyu laut besar, ikan besar, hiu, dan sebagainya. Seekor mo-o bernama Kane-kua-ana konon hidup di Ewabaai (kini Pearl Harbor); menurut penduduk setempat, sekitar 1850 naga itu memindahkan hamparan tiram dari pantai mereka ke bagian laut lain karena marah kepada para pencari tiram, kesimpulan yang mereka tarik ketika tangkapan tiram mulai berkurang.",
      "en": "The mo-o (also called mo-ko) is a fabulous beast (fabeldier), the great sea dragon of Polynesia (Polynesië). In Polynesia a wide range of fauna was once called Mo-O: crocodiles, alligators, eels, large sea turtles, big fish, sharks and so on. A mo-o named Kane-kua-ana was said to live in the Ewabaai (now Pearl Harbor); according to the locals, around 1850 this dragon moved an oyster bed from their coast to another part of the ocean because it was angry with the oyster fishers, a conclusion they drew when the oyster catch began to decline.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mo-o-c01",
        "mo-o-c02",
        "mo-o-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Hawaii, moʻo adalah roh kadal yang dapat berganti wujud, sering mengambil wujud reptil raksasa, tokek kecil, atau manusia. Mereka dihormati sebagai ʻaumakua (leluhur pelindung), dapat menguasai cuaca dan air, bersifat amfibi, dan banyak kolam ikan di Hawaii dipercaya menjadi rumah seekor moʻo. Bila moʻo mati, tubuhnya yang membatu menjadi bagian dari bentang alam. Dalam kisah Hiʻiaka, dewi itu membunuh moʻo pemakan manusia Kikipua, yang lidahnya menyamar sebagai jembatan papan di atas jurang di Molokaʻi.",
      "en": "In Hawaiian mythology the moʻo are shapeshifting lizard spirits, often taking the form of monstrous reptiles, tiny geckos or humans. They were revered as ʻaumakua (ancestral guardians), could have power over weather and water, lived both in water and on land, and many Hawaiian fishponds were thought to shelter one. A dead moʻo turns to stone and becomes a feature of the land. In the tale of Hiʻiaka, the goddess kills the man-eating moʻo Kikipua, whose tongue posed as a plank bridge across a ravine on Molokaʻi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mo-o-c04",
        "mo-o-c05",
        "mo-o-c06",
        "mo-o-c07",
        "mo-o-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "en": "People around Pearl Harbor believed a mo-o named Kane-kua-ana moved their oyster bed around 1850 out of anger.",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c01",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s1",
      "quote": "De mo-o (ook wel mo-ko genoemd) is een fabeldier; de grote zeedraak van Polynesië.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mo-o (juga mo-ko) adalah hewan dongeng (fabeldier), naga laut besar (grote zeedraak) dari Polinesia (Polynesië).",
        "en": "The mo-o (also mo-ko) is a fabulous beast (fabeldier), the great sea dragon (grote zeedraak) of Polynesia (Polynesië)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c02",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s1",
      "quote": "In Polynesia werd vroeger een brede verzameling fauna betiteld als Mo-O; krokodillen, alligators, alen, grote zeeschildpadden, grote vissen, haaien enzovoorts.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Polinesia (Polynesia) berbagai fauna disebut Mo-O: buaya (krokodillen), aligator, belut (alen), penyu laut (zeeschildpadden), ikan besar (vissen), hiu (haaien).",
        "en": "In Polynesia many fauna were called Mo-O: crocodiles (krokodillen), alligators, eels (alen), sea turtles (zeeschildpadden), big fish (vissen), sharks (haaien)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c03",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s1",
      "quote": "Een mo-o met de naam Kane-kua-ana zou geleefd hebben in de Ewabaai (nu Pearl Harbor). Volgens de plaatselijke bevolking zou deze draak rond 1850 een oesterbed van hun kust naar een ander stuk van de oceaan hebben verplaatst, omdat hij boos was geworden op de oestervissers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mo-o bernama Kane-kua-ana hidup di Ewabaai (kini Pearl Harbor); sekitar 1850 naga (draak) itu memindahkan hamparan tiram (oesterbed) karena marah (boos) kepada pencari tiram (oestervissers).",
        "en": "A mo-o named Kane-kua-ana lived in Ewabaai (now Pearl Harbor); around 1850 the dragon (draak) moved an oyster bed (oesterbed) because it was angry (boos) with the oyster fishers (oestervissers)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c04",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s2",
      "quote": "Moʻo are shapeshifting lizard spirits in Hawaiian mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moʻo adalah roh kadal (lizard spirits) yang berganti wujud dalam mitologi Hawaii (Hawaiian).",
        "en": "Moʻo are shapeshifting lizard spirits in Hawaiian mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c05",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s2",
      "quote": "Moʻo often take the forms of monstrous reptiles, tiny geckos, and/or humans. They were revered as ʻaumakua, and could have power over the weather and water. They were amphibious, and many fishponds in Hawaii were believed to be home to a moʻo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moʻo berwujud reptil raksasa, tokek kecil, atau manusia; dihormati sebagai ʻaumakua, menguasai cuaca dan air, dan kolam ikan di Hawaii dipercaya rumah moʻo.",
        "en": "Moʻo take the forms of monstrous reptiles, tiny geckos or humans; revered as ʻaumakua, with power over weather and water; many fishponds in Hawaii were home to a moʻo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c06",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s2",
      "quote": "When a moʻo dies, its petrified body becomes part of the landscape.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila moʻo mati, tubuhnya yang membatu (petrified) menjadi bagian bentang alam (landscape).",
        "en": "When a moʻo dies, its petrified body becomes part of the landscape."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c07",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s2",
      "quote": "Wahineʻomaʻo starts across, but Hiʻiaka recognizes the bewitched bridge for what it is: the tongue of the man-eating moʻo Kikipua. Spanning the gorge with her magical paʻu (skirt), Hiʻiaka chases the lizard to its lair and kills it",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hiʻiaka mengenali jembatan sihir itu sebagai lidah moʻo pemakan manusia Kikipua, lalu mengejar dan membunuhnya; Wahineʻomaʻo hampir menyeberanginya.",
        "en": "Hiʻiaka recognizes the bewitched bridge as the tongue of the man-eating moʻo Kikipua, chases it and kills it; Wahineʻomaʻo had started across."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mo-o-c08",
      "source_id": "mo-o-s2",
      "quote": "On the windward cliffs of Molokaʻi, the young goddess and her attendant Wahineʻomaʻo come to an impassable ravine.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di tebing Molokaʻi, Hiʻiaka dan pengiringnya Wahineʻomaʻo tiba di jurang yang tak terseberangi.",
        "en": "On the windward cliffs of Molokaʻi, Hiʻiaka and her attendant Wahineʻomaʻo reach an impassable ravine."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

    {

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Moongarm adalah monster serigala dari mitologi Nordik (nordijske mitologije) yang akan menelan bulan dan menodai langit dengan darah.",
    "en": "Moongarm is a wolf monster of Norse (nordijske) mythology who will swallow the moon and stain the sky with blood.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "moongarm-c01",
      "moongarm-c02",
      "moongarm-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Moongarm adalah monster (čudovište) dari mitologi Nordik. Ia disebut sebagai serigala paling mengerikan, yang suatu hari akan dibesarkan, memakan darah manusia, dan memercikkan darah itu ke langit.",
      "en": "Moongarm is a monster (čudovište) of Norse mythology. He is named as the most terrible wolf, who will one day be reared, feed on human blood and spatter the sky with it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moongarm-c01",
        "moongarm-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Edda Muda, seorang nenek sihir tinggal di timur Midgard, di hutan Jarnved (Hutan Besi), dan melahirkan banyak putra raksasa yang semuanya berwujud serigala. Yang paling perkasa di antara bangsa serigala itu disebut Moongarm (dalam terjemahan lain Managarm). Ia dipenuhi darah kehidupan orang-orang yang mati, akan melahap bulan, dan menodai langit dengan darah; karenanya matahari akan menjadi gelap dan angin mengamuk.",
      "en": "In the Younger Edda, a hag dwells east of Midgard in the forest Jarnved (Ironwood) and bears many giant sons, all shaped like wolves. The mightiest of that wolf-race is called Moongarm (Managarm in another translation). He is filled with the life-blood of the dead, will devour the moon and stain the sky with blood; then the sun will darken and the winds rage.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "moongarm-c03",
        "moongarm-c04",
        "moongarm-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "moongarm-c01",
      "source_id": "moongarm-s1",
      "quote": "Moongarm je čudovište iz nordijske mitologije.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Moongarm adalah monster (čudovište) dari mitologi Nordik (nordijske mitologije).",
        "en": "Moongarm is a monster (čudovište) of Norse mythology (nordijske mitologije)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moongarm-c02",
      "source_id": "moongarm-s1",
      "quote": "Spominje se kao najstrašniji vuk koji će jednog dana biti uzgojen, hranit će se ljudskom krvlju i špricati nebo s njom.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut serigala (vuk) paling mengerikan yang akan memakan darah manusia (ljudskom krvlju) dan memercikkannya ke langit (nebo).",
        "en": "He is named the most terrible wolf (vuk) who will feed on human blood (ljudskom krvlju) and spatter the sky (nebo) with it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moongarm-c03",
      "source_id": "moongarm-s2",
      "quote": "A hag dwells east of Midgard, in the forest called Jarnved (Ironwood), where reside the witches called Jarnvidjes. The old hag gives birth to many giant sons, and all in wolf’s likeness.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edda Muda (Younger Edda): Seorang nenek sihir tinggal di timur Midgard, di hutan Jarnved (Ironwood, Hutan Besi), dan melahirkan banyak putra raksasa berwujud serigala.",
        "en": "Younger Edda: A hag dwells east of Midgard in the forest Jarnved (Ironwood) and bears many giant sons in wolf's likeness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moongarm-c04",
      "source_id": "moongarm-s2",
      "quote": "It is said that of this wolf-race one is the mightiest, and is called Moongarm. He is filled with the life-blood of all dead men. He will devour the moon, and stain the heavens and all the sky with blood.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edda Muda (Younger Edda): Yang terkuat dari bangsa serigala itu disebut Moongarm; ia dipenuhi darah orang mati, akan melahap bulan, dan menodai langit dengan darah.",
        "en": "Younger Edda: The mightiest of this wolf-race is called Moongarm; he is filled with the life-blood of the dead, will devour the moon and stain the sky with blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moongarm-c05",
      "source_id": "moongarm-s3",
      "quote": "There is one of that race, who is said to be the most formidable of all, called Managarm: he will be filled with the life-blood of men who draw near their end, and will swallow up the moon",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Blackwell menyebutnya Managarm, yang paling menakutkan, dipenuhi darah orang yang menjelang ajal dan akan menelan bulan.",
        "en": "Blackwell's translation calls him Managarm, the most formidable, filled with the life-blood of men near their end, who will swallow the moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "moongarm-c06",
      "source_id": "moongarm-s2",
      "quote": "In the east dwells the old hag, In the Jarnved forest; And brings forth there Fenrer’s offspring.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Syair: di timur tinggal nenek sihir, di hutan Jarnved, yang melahirkan keturunan Fenrer (Fenrir).",
        "en": "Verse: in the east the old hag dwells in the Jarnved forest and brings forth Fenrer's (Fenrir's) offspring."
      }
    }
  ],

        "munin-c02"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Munin adalah salah satu dari dua burung gagak (ravnene) dewa Odin dalam mitologi Nordik; namanya berarti \"ingatan\".",
    "en": "Munin is one of the two ravens (ravnene) of the god Odin in Norse mythology; the name means \"memory\".",
    "claim_ids": [
      "munin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hugin dan Munin dalam mitologi Nordik (norrøn mytologi) adalah nama dua burung gagak milik dewa Odin. Kedua kata Nordik kuno itu berarti \"pikiran\" dan \"ingatan\". Setiap pagi ketika fajar menyingsing, Hugin dan Munin terbang ke seluruh dunia, di atas Åsgard dan Midgard, mengamati apa yang terjadi dan mendengar apa yang dikatakan. Pada akhir hari mereka kembali, hinggap di bahu Odin, dan membisikkan ke telinganya apa yang mereka lihat dan dengar; inilah salah satu sebab Odin menjadi dewa yang paling bijak.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology (norrøn mytologi) Hugin and Munin are the names of the god Odin's two ravens. The two Old Norse words mean \"thought\" and \"memory\". Every morning at daybreak Hugin and Munin fly out over the world, over Åsgard and Midgard, noting what happens and hearing what is said. At day's end they return, perch on Odin's shoulders and whisper in his ear what they have seen and heard; this is one reason Odin is the wisest of the gods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "munin-c01",
        "munin-c02",
        "munin-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Edda Muda menyebut bahwa karena kedua gagak itu Odin dijuluki Rafnagud (dewa gagak). Dalam syair Grímnismál yang dikutipnya, Odin berkata bahwa Hugin dan Munin terbang setiap hari di atas bumi yang luas; ia khawatir Hugin tak kembali, tetapi lebih cemas lagi akan Munin.",
      "en": "The Younger Edda says that because of the two ravens Odin is called Rafnagud (raven-god). In the Grímnismál verse it quotes, Odin says Hugin and Munin fly every day over the wide earth; he fears Hugin may not return, but is more anxious still for Munin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "munin-c04",
        "munin-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": {

  "did_you_know": {
    "id": "Dalam Edda, Odin berkata ia khawatir Hugin tak kembali, tetapi lebih mencemaskan Munin, gagak \"ingatan\"-nya.",
    "en": "In the Edda, Odin says he fears Hugin may not return, but worries even more for Munin, his raven of \"memory\".",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "munin-c01",
      "source_id": "munin-s1",
      "quote": "Hugin og Munin er i norrøn mytologi navnet på de to ravnene til guden Odin. De to gammelnordiske ordene betyr «Tanken» og «Minnet».",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (norrøn mytologi) Hugin dan Munin adalah dua gagak (ravnene) dewa Odin; kedua kata Nordik kuno (gammelnordiske, Old Norse) itu berarti pikiran (Tanken) dan ingatan (Minnet).",
        "en": "In Norse mythology (norrøn mytologi) Hugin and Munin are the god Odin's two ravens (ravnene); the two Old Norse (gammelnordiske) words mean thought (Tanken) and memory (Minnet)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "munin-c02",
      "source_id": "munin-s1",
      "quote": "Hver morgen når dagen gryr, flyr Hugin og Munin ut i verden. De flyr både over Åsgard og Midgard. De legger merke til det som skjer og de hører hva som blir sagt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setiap pagi (morgen) Hugin dan Munin terbang (flyr) ke dunia (verden), di atas Åsgard dan Midgard, mengamati dan mendengar apa yang dikatakan.",
        "en": "Every morning (morgen) Hugin and Munin fly (flyr) out into the world (verden), over Åsgard and Midgard, noting and hearing what is said."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "munin-c03",
      "source_id": "munin-s1",
      "quote": "På slutten av dagen kommer de tilbake og setter seg på skuldrene til Odin. De hvisker inn i øret til Odin hva de har sett og hørt både fra menneskenes og gudenes verden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada akhir hari mereka hinggap di bahu (skuldrene) Odin dan membisikkan (hvisker) ke telinganya (øret) apa yang mereka lihat dan dengar.",
        "en": "At day's end they sit on Odin's shoulders (skuldrene) and whisper (hvisker) into his ear (øret) what they have seen and heard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "munin-c04",
      "source_id": "munin-s2",
      "quote": "Two ravens sit on Odin’s shoulders, and bring to his ears all that they hear and see. Their names are Hugin and Munin. At dawn he sends them out to fly over the whole world, and they come back at breakfast time. Thus he gets information about many things, and hence he is called Rafnagud (raven-god).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edda Muda (Younger Edda): Dua gagak di bahu Odin, Hugin dan Munin, terbang ke seluruh dunia saat fajar; karena itu Odin disebut Rafnagud (dewa gagak).",
        "en": "Younger Edda: Two ravens on Odin's shoulders, Hugin and Munin, fly over the world at dawn; hence Odin is called Rafnagud (raven-god)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "munin-c05",
      "source_id": "munin-s3",
      "quote": "Hugin and Munin fly each day over the spacious earth. I fear for Hugin, that he come not back, yet more anxious am I for Munin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edda, Grímnismál: Hugin dan Munin terbang setiap hari di atas bumi; Odin khawatir Hugin tak kembali, lebih cemas lagi akan Munin.",
        "en": "Edda, Grímnismál: Hugin and Munin fly each day over the earth; Odin fears Hugin may not return, yet is more anxious for Munin."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Nabbi is a dwarf (Zwerg) of Norse mythology who, with the dwarf Dain, made the golden boar Hildisvini of the goddess Freyja.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nabbi adalah kurcaci (Zwerg) dalam mitologi Nordik, yang bersama kurcaci Dain membuat babi hutan emas Hildisvini milik dewi Freyja. Dalam Hyndluljóð, Freyja berbicara dengan raksasa perempuan Hyndla tentang penciptaan Hildisvini oleh kedua kurcaci itu: ia berkata Hyndla bermimpi bila mengira suaminya ada di jalan orang mati, padahal yang ada di sana adalah babi berbulu emas yang berkilau, Hildisvini, buatan dua kurcaci yang terampil, Dain dan Nabbi.",
      "en": "Nabbi is a dwarf (Zwerg) of Norse mythology who, together with the dwarf Dain, made the golden boar Hildisvini of the goddess Freyja. In Hyndluljóð Freyja speaks with the giantess Hyndla about the making of Hildisvini by the two dwarfs: she says Hyndla is dreaming if she thinks her man is on the road of the dead, for there is the shining gold-bristled boar Hildisvini, made by two skilful dwarfs, Dain and Nabbi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nabbi-c01",
        "nabbi-c02",
        "nabbi-c03",
        "nabbi-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Viktor Rydberg dalam Teutonic Mythology menyamakan Nabbi dengan kurcaci Dvalin dan Sindri.",
      "en": "In his Teutonic Mythology, Viktor Rydberg equates Nabbi with the dwarfs Dvalin and Sindri.",
      "claim_ids": [

      ]
    }

    "id": "Ægir (juga Hlér atau Gymir) adalah jötunn dan personifikasi laut dalam mitologi Nordik, tuan rumah pesta para dewa yang dikaitkan dengan pembuatan bir.",
    "en": "Ægir (also Hlér or Gymir) is a jötunn and personification of the sea in Norse mythology, host of the gods' feasts and associated with brewing ale.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "norse-sea-gods-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Ægir (dieja Aegir; dalam Old Norse berarti \"laut\"), Hlér, atau Gymir adalah jötunn dan personifikasi laut. Dalam sumber Old Norse ia menjamu para dewa di aulanya dan dikaitkan dengan pembuatan bir. Bersama istrinya Rán, dewi yang juga mempersonifikasikan laut, ia memiliki sembilan putri yang mempersonifikasikan ombak, dan putranya adalah Snær, personifikasi salju. Dalam puisi skald, kapal disebut \"kuda Ægir\" dan ombak \"putri-putri Ægir\".",
      "en": "In Norse mythology, Ægir (anglicised Aegir; Old Norse for \"sea\"), also known as Hlér or Gymir, is a jötunn who embodies the sea. The Old Norse texts show him entertaining the gods in his halls and link him with brewing ale. With his wife Rán, a goddess who also embodies the sea, he has nine daughters who embody the waves, and his son is Snær, who embodies snow. In skaldic poetry a ship is \"Ægir's horse\" and the waves are the \"daughters of Ægir\".",
      "claim_ids": [
        "norse-sea-gods-c01",
        "norse-sea-gods-c02",
        "norse-sea-gods-c16"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Hymiskviða, para dewa yang haus setelah berburu mendapati bahwa di tempat Ægir tersedia banyak kuali; Ægir yang kesal meminta Thor mengambilkan kuali yang sangat besar agar ia dapat menyeduh bir untuk mereka semua. Menurut pengantar prosa Lokasenna, Ægir, yang juga disebut Gymir, mengadakan pesta dengan kuali besar itu; Loki membunuh pelayan Ægir, Fimafeng, lalu kembali ke aula dan memicu pertengkaran.",
      "en": "In Hymiskviða the gods, thirsty after a hunt, find that Ægir has plenty of cauldrons; the annoyed Ægir asks Thor to fetch an enormous cauldron so he can brew ale for them all. According to the prose introduction to Lokasenna, Ægir, also called Gymir, hosts a feast with that great cauldron; Loki kills Ægir's servant Fimafeng, then returns to the hall and stirs up strife.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "norse-sea-gods-c03",
        "norse-sea-gods-c04",
        "norse-sea-gods-c05",
        "norse-sea-gods-c17"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Edda Prosa, Ægir atau Hler tinggal di pulau Hler (Hler's Isle, kini Læsø), dan mahir dalam sihir; ia berkunjung ke Asgard, duduk di samping dewa puisi Brage, dan mendengar kisah-kisah para dewa. Kenning \"api Ægir\" untuk emas berasal dari kisah bahwa Ægir menerangi aulanya dengan emas yang menyala. Rán, istrinya, memiliki jala untuk menangkap semua orang yang melaut.",
      "en": "In the Prose Edda, Ægir or Hler lives on Hler's Isle, now Læsø, and is skilled in magic; he visits Asgard, sits beside the poet-god Brage and hears the tales of the gods. The kenning \"Ægir's fire\" for gold comes from the story that Ægir lit his hall with glowing gold. Rán, his wife, had a net in which she caught everyone who went to sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "norse-sea-gods-c06",
        "norse-sea-gods-c07",
        "norse-sea-gods-c08",
        "norse-sea-gods-c09",
        "norse-sea-gods-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para sarjana memperdebatkan statusnya. Anthony Faulkes mencatat bahwa penulis modern sering menyebutnya dewa laut, padahal Edda Prosa tidak pernah menyebutnya dewa dan memasukkannya ke daftar jötnar; menurut Rudolf Simek, walau tercatat sebagai jötunn, Ægir memiliki ciri-ciri dewa laut. Mineral aegirine dan eksoplanet Epsilon Eridani b dinamai menurut Ægir.",
      "en": "Scholars debate his status. Anthony Faulkes notes that modern writers often call him a god of the sea, though the Prose Edda never calls him a god and lists him among the jötnar; according to Rudolf Simek, while attested as a jötunn, Ægir has the traits of a sea god. The mineral aegirine and the exoplanet Epsilon Eridani b are named after Ægir.",

        "norse-sea-gods-c12",
        "norse-sea-gods-c13",
        "norse-sea-gods-c15"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c01",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, Ægir (anglicised as Aegir, Old Norse: 'sea'), Hlér (Old Norse: 'sea'), or Gymir (Old Norse less clearly: 'sea' or 'engulfer'), is a jötunn and personification of the sea. In the Old Norse record, Ægir hosts the gods in his halls and is associated with brewing ale.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (Norse), Ægir (Aegir), Hlér, atau Gymir adalah jötunn dan personifikasi laut (Old Norse 'sea') yang menjamu dewa dan dikaitkan dengan bir (ale).",
        "en": "In Norse mythology Ægir (Aegir), Hlér or Gymir is a jötunn and personification of the sea (Old Norse 'sea') who hosts the gods and is associated with brewing ale."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c02",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "For instance, a ship is described as \"Ægir's horse\" and the waves as the \"daughters of Ægir\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam puisi skald kapal disebut \"kuda Ægir\" (Ægir's horse) dan ombak \"putri Ægir\" (daughters of Ægir).",
        "en": "In skaldic poetry a ship is \"Ægir's horse\" and the waves the \"daughters of Ægir\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c03",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "In Hymiskviða, Ægir plays a major role. In the poem, the gods have become thirsty after a successful hunt, and are keen to celebrate with drink.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Hymiskviða, para dewa haus setelah berburu dan ingin berpesta minum di tempat Ægir.",
        "en": "In Hymiskviða the gods are thirsty after a hunt and keen to celebrate with drink at Ægir's."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c04",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "He asks Thor to fetch a particular cauldron, and that with it he could brew ale for them all.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ægir meminta Thor mengambil kuali (cauldron) tertentu agar ia dapat menyeduh bir untuk semua.",
        "en": "Ægir asks Thor to fetch a particular cauldron so he can brew ale for them all."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c05",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "According to the prose introduction to Lokasenna, \"Ægir, who is also called Gymir\", was hosting a feast \"with the great cauldron which has just been told about\", which many of the gods and elves attended.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut pengantar prosa Lokasenna, Ægir yang juga disebut Gymir mengadakan pesta dengan kuali besar, dihadiri banyak dewa dan elf.",
        "en": "According to the prose introduction to Lokasenna, Ægir, also called Gymir, hosted a feast with the great cauldron, attended by many gods and elves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c06",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s2",
      "quote": "A man by name Æger, or Hler, who dwelt on the island called Hler’s Isle, was well skilled in the black art. He made a journey to Asgard.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Edda Prosa (terj. Anderson): Æger atau Hler, yang tinggal di pulau Hler (Hler's Isle), mahir ilmu hitam dan berkunjung ke Asgard.",
        "en": "Prose Edda (Anderson): Æger or Hler, living on Hler's Isle, was skilled in the black art and journeyed to Asgard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c07",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "As highlighted above in Skáldskaparmál, the name of the island Læsø in Denmark references Hlér (Old Norse Hléysey 'Hlér's Island').",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama pulau Læsø di Denmark merujuk Hlér (Hléysey, pulau Hlér), menurut Skáldskaparmál.",
        "en": "The name of the island Læsø in Denmark references Hlér (Hléysey, Hlér's Island), per Skáldskaparmál."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c08",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "Chapter 33b of Skáldskaparmál discusses why skalds may refer to gold as \"Ægir's fire\". The section traces the kenning to a narrative surrounding Ægir, in which the jötunn employs \"glowing gold\" in the center of his hall to light it \"like fire\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Skáldskaparmál menjelaskan kenning \"api Ægir\" (Ægir's fire) untuk emas: Ægir menerangi aulanya dengan emas menyala (glowing gold).",
        "en": "Skáldskaparmál explains the kenning \"Ægir's fire\" for gold: Ægir lit his hall with glowing gold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c09",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "Then the Æsir discovered that Ran had a net in which she caught everyone that went to sea",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para Æsir mengetahui bahwa Rán (Ran) memiliki jala untuk menangkap semua orang yang melaut.",
        "en": "The Æsir discovered that Ran had a net to catch everyone who went to sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c10",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s2",
      "quote": "Next to Æger sat Brage, and they talked much together over their drink. Brage spoke to Æger of many things that had happened to the asas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brage duduk di samping Æger dan menceritakan banyak kisah para asas (dewa).",
        "en": "Brage sat next to Æger and told him many things that had happened to the asas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c11",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "Anthony Faulkes observes that Ægir is \"often described by modern writers as god of the sea\" yet that he is nowhere described as a god in the Prose Edda and appears in a list of jötnar in Skáldskaparmál.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anthony Faulkes mencatat Ægir sering disebut dewa laut oleh penulis modern, tetapi Prose Edda tak pernah menyebutnya dewa dan memasukkannya ke daftar jötnar.",
        "en": "Anthony Faulkes notes Ægir is often called a sea god by modern writers, yet the Prose Edda never calls him a god and lists him among the jötnar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c12",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "According to Rudolf Simek, while attested as a jötunn, Ægir \"has characteristics\" of a sea god.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Rudolf Simek, walau tercatat sebagai jötunn, Ægir memiliki ciri dewa laut (sea god).",
        "en": "According to Rudolf Simek, though attested as a jötunn, Ægir has characteristics of a sea god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c13",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "The mineral aegirine, first described from samples in Kongsberg, Norway, in 1821, was named after Ægir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mineral aegirine, pertama dideskripsikan dari Kongsberg, Norwegia (Norway) pada 1821, dinamai menurut Ægir.",
        "en": "The mineral aegirine, first described from Kongsberg, Norway, in 1821, was named after Ægir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c14",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "These include Swedish artist Nils Blommér's painting Näcken och Ägirs döttrar (1850)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karya seni tentang Ægir mencakup lukisan Nils Blommér (seniman Swedia, Swedish) Näcken och Ägirs döttrar (1850).",
        "en": "Art about Ægir includes Swedish artist Nils Blommér's painting Näcken och Ägirs döttrar (1850)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c15",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "An exoplanet, Epsilon Eridani b, discovered in 2000, was formally named Ægir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Eksoplanet Epsilon Eridani b, ditemukan tahun 2000, secara resmi dinamai Ægir.",
        "en": "The exoplanet Epsilon Eridani b, discovered in 2000, was formally named Ægir."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c16",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "Ægir and his wife Rán, a goddess who also personifies the sea, and together the two produced nine daughters who personify the sea waves, and Ægir's son is Snær, who personifies the snow.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ægir dan istrinya Rán, dewi yang juga mempersonifikasikan laut, memiliki sembilan putri (nine daughters) personifikasi ombak; putra Ægir adalah Snær, personifikasi salju (snow).",
        "en": "Ægir and his wife Rán, a goddess who also personifies the sea, had nine daughters personifying the waves; Ægir's son is Snær, who personifies snow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "norse-sea-gods-c17",
      "source_id": "norse-sea-gods-s1",
      "quote": "The gods praise the excellence of their service and, hearing this, Loki murders Fimafeng, enraging the gods, who chase him out to the woods before returning to drink.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para dewa memuji pelayanan pelayan Ægir; mendengar itu Loki membunuh Fimafeng, sehingga para dewa marah dan mengusirnya.",
        "en": "The gods praise the servants' service; hearing this, Loki murders Fimafeng, and the angry gods chase him out."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Okesa neko (おけさ猫, \"Okesa si kucing\") adalah tokoh legenda Jepang tentang seekor bakeneko yang menjelma geisha muda cantik dan bernyanyi untuk melunasi utang bekas pemiliknya.",
    "en": "Okesa neko (おけさ猫, \"Okesa the cat\") is a figure of Japanese legend: a bakeneko who became a pretty young geisha and sang to pay off her former owner's debts.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "okesa-neko-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Okesa neko adalah tokoh legenda dalam cerita rakyat Jepang (japanischen Folklore) tentang seekor Bakeneko yang, sebagai geisha muda yang cantik, berkeliling Jepang sambil bernyanyi dan bermain musik untuk melunasi utang bekas pemiliknya. Legenda tertua berasal dari provinsi Sado: di pulau Sado tinggal seorang nenek pencinta kucing yang memelihara puluhan kucing, tetapi ketika hartanya habis kucing-kucing itu pergi satu per satu, hingga kucing kesayangannya pun lenyap.",
      "en": "Okesa neko is a figure of Japanese folklore (japanischen Folklore): a Bakeneko who, as a pretty young geisha, travelled through Japan singing and playing music to pay off her former owner's debts. The oldest legend comes from Sado province: on the island of Sado lived an old woman who kept dozens of cats, but when her money ran out they left one by one, until even her favourite disappeared.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "okesa-neko-c01",
        "okesa-neko-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Suatu pagi seorang gadis cantik datang mengetuk pintu; kimononya berpola dan berwarna persis seperti bulu kucing kesayangan si nenek. Gadis itu memperkenalkan diri sebagai Okesa dan berjanji mengembalikan harta si nenek. Di Edo ia menjadi geisha yang begitu indah suaranya hingga burung pun terdiam karena iri. Namun seorang kapten kapal memergokinya dalam wujud kucing raksasa yang memakan kepala ikan; Okesa mengancamnya agar tutup mulut, dan ketika ia membocorkan rahasia itu, Okesa dalam wujud kucing raksasa mencelupkannya berulang kali ke air lalu membawanya pergi.",
      "en": "One morning a pretty girl knocked at the door; her kimono had exactly the pattern and colour of the old woman's favourite cat's fur. The girl introduced herself as Okesa and promised to win back the old woman's money. In Edo she became a geisha whose singing was so beautiful that even the birds fell silent with envy. But a ship's captain caught her as a giant cat eating fish heads; Okesa threatened him to keep quiet, and when he let the secret slip, Okesa in giant cat form dunked him repeatedly in the water and carried him off.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "okesa-neko-c03",
        "okesa-neko-c04",
        "okesa-neko-c05",
        "okesa-neko-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Okesa berasal dari aliran musik Okesa bushi yang muncul pada awal zaman Edo dan berasal dari pulau Sado; kisah ini juga dikenal sebagai \"Geisha penyanyi dari Sado\" dan masuk ke teater Nō. Okesa yang baik hati tidak biasa bagi Bakeneko, yang umumnya digambarkan licik dan pendendam, sehingga sebagian folkloris menduga ia terilhami oleh Maneki-neko yang membawa keberuntungan.",
      "en": "The name Okesa comes from the Okesa bushi style of music, which arose early in the Edo period and originated on Sado island; the tale is also known as \"The singing geisha of Sado\" and even entered Nō theatre. A kind-hearted Okesa is unusual for a Bakeneko, usually described as treacherous and vengeful, so some folklorists suspect she was inspired by the lucky Maneki-neko.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c01",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Okesa neko (おけさ猫; „Okesa, die Katze“) ist eine Sagengestalt der japanischen Folklore um eine Bakeneko, die als hübsche, junge Geisha singend und musizierend durch Japan gezogen sei, um die Schulden ihrer ehemaligen Besitzerin abbezahlen zu können.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Okesa neko adalah tokoh legenda (Sagengestalt) cerita rakyat Jepang (japanischen Folklore): Bakeneko yang sebagai geisha muda (junge Geisha) bernyanyi keliling Jepang untuk melunasi utang (Schulden) bekas pemiliknya.",
        "en": "Okesa neko is a legendary figure (Sagengestalt) of Japanese folklore (japanischen Folklore): a Bakeneko who as a young geisha (junge Geisha) sang through Japan to pay off the debts (Schulden) of her former owner."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c02",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Die älteste überlieferte Legende stammt aus der Provinz Sado. Der Sage nach lebte auf der Insel Sado eine alte Frau, die als Katzensammlerin bekannt war. Sie besaß dutzende von ihnen und sie kümmerte sich aufopfernd um ihre Tiere.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda tertua (älteste Legende) berasal dari provinsi Sado; di pulau (Insel) Sado tinggal nenek (alte Frau) pengumpul kucing (Katzensammlerin) yang memelihara puluhan kucing.",
        "en": "The oldest legend (älteste Legende) comes from Sado province; on the island (Insel) of Sado lived an old woman (alte Frau) known as a cat collector (Katzensammlerin) with dozens of cats."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c03",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Der alten Frau fiel sogleich auf, dass der edle Kimono, den das Mädchen trug, exakt dasselbe Muster und dieselbe Farbe hatte, wie das Fell ihrer einstigen Lieblingskatze. Das Mädchen stellte sich als „Okesa“ vor",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kimono gadis (Mädchen) itu berpola (Muster) dan berwarna (Farbe) sama dengan bulu (Fell) kucing kesayangan (Lieblingskatze); gadis itu memperkenalkan diri sebagai Okesa.",
        "en": "The girl's (Mädchen) kimono had the same pattern (Muster) and colour (Farbe) as the fur (Fell) of the favourite cat (Lieblingskatze); she introduced herself as Okesa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c04",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Schon bald darauf ging ein erstauntes Raunen und Murmeln in ganz Edo um eine neue Geisha um, die so schön sei und so gut singen könne, dass selbst die Vögel vor Neid verstummten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di seluruh Edo beredar kabar tentang geisha baru yang suaranya begitu indah hingga burung (Vögel) terdiam karena iri (Neid).",
        "en": "All Edo murmured of a new geisha who sang so well that even the birds (Vögel) fell silent with envy (Neid)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c05",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "auf dem Bett, gekleidet in Okesas Kimono, saß eine riesige Katze und verzehrte die Fischköpfe des vergangenen Abends. Die Katze starrte ihn eiskalt an und sprach: „Verrate mich und ich komme über Dich!“",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di ranjang (Bett) duduk kucing raksasa (riesige Katze) berkimono Okesa yang memakan kepala ikan (Fischköpfe) dan mengancam sang kapten agar tak membocorkannya (Verrate mich).",
        "en": "On the bed (Bett) sat a giant cat (riesige Katze) in Okesa's kimono eating fish heads (Fischköpfe), and it threatened the captain not to betray it (Verrate mich)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c06",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Plötzlich erschien Okesa in Gestalt einer riesigen Katze, packte ihn und tunkte ihn mehrfach im Wasser auf und ab.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Okesa muncul dalam wujud kucing raksasa (riesigen Katze), menangkap kapten, dan mencelupkannya berulang kali ke air (Wasser).",
        "en": "Okesa appeared as a giant cat (riesigen Katze), seized the captain and dunked him repeatedly in the water (Wasser)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c07",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Der Name, den die Sagengestalt angenommen haben soll, rührt von einer Musikrichtung namens Okesa bushi (おけさ節: „Okesa-Stil“) her, die zu Beginn der Edo-Zeit im frühen 18. Jahrhundert aufkam und ihren Ursprung in der Provinz Sado auf der gleichnamigen Insel hatte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama (Name) itu berasal dari aliran musik (Musikrichtung) Okesa bushi yang muncul pada zaman Edo (Edo-Zeit) dan berasal dari provinsi Sado.",
        "en": "The name (Name) comes from the music style (Musikrichtung) Okesa bushi, arising in the Edo period (Edo-Zeit) and originating in Sado province."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c08",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Dass mit Okesa eine gutherzige Bakeneko auftritt, ist eher ungewöhnlich, da Bakeneko für gewöhnlich als heimtückisch und rachsüchtig beschrieben werden. Deshalb mutmaßen manche Folkloristen, dass Okesa eher von der gutartigen und glückbringenden Maneki-neko inspiriert worden sein könnte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Okesa sebagai Bakeneko baik hati (gutherzige) tidak biasa, karena Bakeneko biasanya licik (heimtückisch) dan pendendam; folkloris (Folkloristen) menduga ia terilhami Maneki-neko.",
        "en": "A kind-hearted (gutherzige) Bakeneko like Okesa is unusual, as Bakeneko are usually treacherous (heimtückisch) and vengeful; some folklorists (Folkloristen) suspect inspiration from Maneki-neko."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c09",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s2",
      "quote": "Sie ist ein Katzendämon aus der Gruppe der Yōkai und gilt als Verwandte der Nekomata („Gegabelte Katze“). Bakenekos werden als bösartig beschrieben und gelten, wie die Nekomata, als das Gegenstück zur Glück bringenden Maneki-neko („Winke-Katze“).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bakeneko adalah iblis kucing (Katzendämon) golongan Yōkai, digambarkan jahat (bösartig), dan dianggap kebalikan (Gegenstück) dari Maneki-neko pembawa untung.",
        "en": "The Bakeneko is a cat demon (Katzendämon) of the Yōkai group, described as malicious (bösartig) and seen as the counterpart (Gegenstück) of the lucky Maneki-neko."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "okesa-neko-c10",
      "source_id": "okesa-neko-s1",
      "quote": "Die Sage ist bis heute auch unter dem Titel „Die singende Geisha von Sado“ bekannt und beliebt und fand sogar Eingang ins traditionelle Nō-Theater.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah ini juga dikenal sebagai \"Die singende Geisha von Sado\" dan masuk ke teater Nō (Nō-Theater) tradisional.",
        "en": "The tale is also known as \"Die singende Geisha von Sado\" and entered traditional Nō theatre (Nō-Theater)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Paradijsvogel (manucodiata, \"Vogels van God\") adalah burung cenderawasih yang dulu dianggap makhluk dongeng: tanpa daging dan tulang, tanpa kaki, hidup di surga dan tak pernah perlu mendarat.",
    "en": "The paradijsvogel (manucodiata, \"birds of God\") is the bird of paradise, once credited with fabulous traits: without flesh or bones, without feet, living in paradise and never needing to land.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "paradijsvogel-c01",
      "paradijsvogel-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Burung cenderawasih (paradijsvogel, manucodiata, \"Vogels van God\") adalah hewan nyata, tetapi dulu diberi sifat-sifat dongeng. Ia dikatakan tak berdaging dan tak bertulang, hidup di surga (paradijs) dan makan embun. Ia tak berkaki, melainkan mengaitkan diri ke dahan dengan dua benang dari ekornya; bulunya yang panjang dan bergelombang berkilau keemasan, jingga, zamrud, dan cokelat kastanye, dan ia turun ke bumi hanya untuk beristirahat.",
      "en": "The bird of paradise (paradijsvogel, manucodiata, \"Vogels van God\") is a real animal but was once given fabulous traits. It was said to have no flesh or bones, to live in paradise (paradijs) and feed on dew. It had no feet but hooked itself to a branch with two threads from its tail; its long, wavy plumes shone gold, orange, emerald and chestnut, and it came down to earth only to rest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "paradijsvogel-c01",
        "paradijsvogel-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Konon betinanya bertelur di cekungan punggung jantan, dan telur itu dierami saat sang jantan terbang. Burung ini hanya dapat ditangkap dengan dahan berlem, tetapi mati dalam perangkap itu, sehingga orang baru dapat melihat keindahannya setelah ia mati. Para penguasa memakai bulunya dalam pertempuran karena dianggap sekuat baju zirah. Menurut artikel sv, paradisfåglar dibayangkan selalu terbang tanpa pernah mendarat, berhubungan langsung dengan surga, serta magis dan membawa keberuntungan; kadang mereka disebut sylfer.",
      "en": "The females were said to lay eggs in a hollow in the male's back, hatched while he flew. The bird could only be caught with limed branches, but died in such a trap, so people could see its beauty only once it was dead. Rulers wore its feathers in battle because they were thought as strong as armour. According to the sv article, paradisfåglar were imagined as always in flight and never landing, directly connected with paradise, magical and lucky; they were sometimes called sylfer.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "paradijsvogel-c03",
        "paradijsvogel-c04",
        "paradijsvogel-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda ini diperkuat oleh kulit burung yang dikirim ke Eropa tanpa kaki atau tanpa tulang: pada akhir 1800-an dan awal 1900-an kapal-kapal membawa burung yang dikuliti sedemikian rupa sehingga tubuhnya utuh tanpa kerangka, dan orang menduga burung tanpa kerangka tak perlu mendarat. Sultan Batjan di Maluku menghadiahkan dua burung cenderawasih kepada kapten kapal Victoria untuk raja Spanyol; pada masa Ratu Victoria, dua burung hidup yang diberikan kepada kebun binatang London ternyata burung biasa. Selma Lagerlöf menulis bahwa pertemuannya dengan seekor paradisfågel membuatnya sembuh dari penyakit yang menyulitkannya berjalan.",
      "en": "The legend was fed by bird skins sent to Europe without feet or bones: in the late 1800s and early 1900s ships brought birds skinned so that the body stayed whole but had no skeleton, and people supposed a bird without a skeleton need never land. The sultan of Batjan in the Moluccas gave two birds of paradise to the captain of the Victoria for the Spanish king; under Queen Victoria, two live birds given to the London zoo turned out to be ordinary birds. Selma Lagerlöf wrote that meeting a paradisfågel healed her of an illness that made walking hard for her.",

  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "url": "https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradijsvogel_(fabeldier)",
      "title": "Paradijsvogel (fabeldier)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (nl)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "nl",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-s2",
      "url": "https://sv.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradisf%C3%A5glar_(mytologi)",
      "title": "Paradisfåglar (mytologi)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (sv)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "sv",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c01",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "De paradijsvogel (manucodiata, \"Vogels van God\") is een bestaand dier (zie paradijsvogel), maar het werd vroeger fabelachtige eigenschappen toegeschreven. Het zou een wezen zonder vlees en botten zijn, levend in het paradijs, waar het zich voedt met dauw.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paradijsvogel (manucodiata, Vogels van God) adalah hewan nyata yang dulu diberi sifat dongeng (fabelachtige): tanpa daging (vlees) dan tulang (botten), hidup di surga (paradijs), makan embun (dauw).",
        "en": "The paradijsvogel (manucodiata, Vogels van God) is a real animal once given fabulous (fabelachtige) traits: without flesh (vlees) or bones (botten), living in paradise (paradijs), feeding on dew (dauw)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c02",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "Het heeft geen poten maar haakt zich met twee draden uit zijn staart vast aan een boomtak. Zijn lange, golvende veren zijn ragfijn en stralen goud, oranje, smaragd en kastanjebruin. De vogel daalt alleen af naar de aarde om te rusten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tak berkaki (geen poten), mengait ke dahan (boomtak) dengan benang ekor (staart); bulunya (veren) berkilau emas (goud), jingga, zamrud; ia turun ke bumi (aarde) hanya untuk beristirahat (rusten).",
        "en": "It has no feet (geen poten), hooks onto a branch (boomtak) with tail (staart) threads; its feathers (veren) shine gold (goud), orange, emerald; it comes to earth (aarde) only to rest (rusten)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c03",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "Men zegt dat de vrouwtjes eieren leggen in een holte in de rug van de mannetjes. Ze worden uitgebroed terwijl hij vliegt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon betina (vrouwtjes) bertelur (eieren) di cekungan punggung (rug) jantan, dan telur dierami (uitgebroed) saat ia terbang (vliegt).",
        "en": "The females (vrouwtjes) are said to lay eggs (eieren) in a hollow in the male's back (rug), hatched (uitgebroed) while he flies (vliegt)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c04",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "De vogel is te vangen door takken en riet met lijm in te smeren, maar het dier sterft in zo'n val. Pas als de paradijsvogel dood is, kunnen de mensen zijn schoonheid aanschouwen. In Oost-Indië zouden dode paradijsvogels gevonden zijn. Heersers droegen hun veren bij gevechten, omdat ze zo sterk als een harnas zouden zijn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Burung (vogel) ditangkap dengan dahan berlem (lijm) tetapi mati dalam perangkap (val); keindahannya (schoonheid) baru terlihat setelah mati (dood); penguasa (Heersers) memakai bulunya sekuat zirah (harnas).",
        "en": "The bird (vogel) is caught with limed (lijm) branches but dies in the trap (val); its beauty (schoonheid) is seen only when dead (dood); rulers (Heersers) wore its feathers as strong as armour (harnas)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c05",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "De sultan van Batjan op de Molukken gaf twee paradijsvogels cadeau aan de kapitein van de Victoria als geschenk voor de Spaanse koning.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sultan Batjan di Maluku (Molukken) menghadiahkan dua paradijsvogels kepada kapten (kapitein) kapal Victoria untuk raja Spanyol (Spaanse koning).",
        "en": "The sultan of Batjan in the Moluccas (Molukken) gave two paradijsvogels to the captain (kapitein) of the Victoria for the Spanish king (Spaanse koning)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c06",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "Onder koningin Victoria werden er twee levende paradijsvogels aan de Londense dierentuin geschonken. De Paradisea Apoda (zonder poten) bleken gewone vogels te zijn: paradijsvogels.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada masa Ratu (koningin) Victoria, dua paradijsvogels hidup diberikan ke kebun binatang London (Londense dierentuin) dan ternyata burung biasa (gewone vogels).",
        "en": "Under Queen (koningin) Victoria two live paradijsvogels given to the London zoo (Londense dierentuin) proved to be ordinary birds (gewone vogels)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c07",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s2",
      "quote": "Paradisfåglar är ett mytiskt väsen, fåglar som ständigt befinner sig i flykt och aldrig behöver landa. Dessa skall då stå i direkt förbindelse med paradiset självt, och vara magiska och lyckobringande. Dessa kallades också ibland för sylfer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paradisfåglar adalah makhluk mitos (mytiskt väsen), burung (fåglar) yang selalu terbang (flykt) dan tak pernah mendarat (landa), berhubungan dengan surga (paradiset), magis (magiska), membawa untung (lyckobringande), kadang disebut sylfer.",
        "en": "Paradisfåglar are a mythical being (mytiskt väsen), birds (fåglar) always in flight (flykt) that never land (landa), tied to paradise (paradiset), magical (magiska) and lucky (lyckobringande), sometimes called sylfer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c08",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s2",
      "quote": "Under sent 1800-tal och början av 1900-talet, så kom det lastskepp till Europa från andra kontinenter med fåglar som blivit flådda på ett sätt som lämnade fågelkroppen intakt med fjädrar och allt, dock helt utan skelett.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada akhir 1800-an dan awal 1900-an kapal barang (lastskepp) membawa ke Eropa (Europa) burung yang dikuliti (flådda) utuh dengan bulu (fjädrar) tetapi tanpa kerangka (skelett).",
        "en": "In the late 1800s and early 1900s cargo ships (lastskepp) brought to Europe (Europa) birds skinned (flådda) whole with feathers (fjädrar) but without skeleton (skelett)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c09",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s2",
      "quote": "Författare som skrivit om paradisfåglar som mytologiska väsen, är till exempel Selma Lagerlöf. Hon berättar om sitt möte med en paradisfågel som orsaken till att hon blev helad från en sjukdom som gjorde att hon hade svårt att gå.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selma Lagerlöf menulis bahwa pertemuannya (möte) dengan paradisfågel menyembuhkannya (helad) dari penyakit (sjukdom) yang membuatnya sulit berjalan (gå).",
        "en": "Selma Lagerlöf wrote that her meeting (möte) with a paradisfågel healed (helad) her of an illness (sjukdom) that made it hard to walk (gå)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c10",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "Een beroemde paradijsvogel is Isadora Paradijsvogel uit de Fabeltjeskrant.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paradijsvogel terkenal adalah Isadora Paradijsvogel dari Fabeltjeskrant; Louis Paul Boon menulis novel De Paradijsvogel.",
        "en": "A famous paradijsvogel is Isadora Paradijsvogel from the Fabeltjeskrant; Louis Paul Boon wrote the novel De Paradijsvogel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paradijsvogel-c11",
      "source_id": "paradijsvogel-s1",
      "quote": "Louis Paul Boon schreef de roman De Paradijsvogel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Louis Paul Boon menulis novel (roman) De Paradijsvogel.",
        "en": "Louis Paul Boon wrote the novel (roman) De Paradijsvogel."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hippogryph (Pferdegreif dalam heraldik) adalah makhluk dongeng campuran, separuh griffin dan separuh kuda, yang muncul dalam epos kesatria Italia.",
    "en": "The hippogryph (Pferdegreif in heraldry) is a fabulous hybrid, half griffin and half horse, that appears in Italian chivalric epics.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "pferdegreif-c01",
      "pferdegreif-c09"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hippogryph (dari bahasa Italia ippogrifo, gabungan hippos, \"kuda\", dan kata Italia grifo, \"griffin\") adalah makhluk dongeng campuran yang separuh griffin dan separuh kuda. Menurut artikel de, istilah ini adalah ciptaan Matteo Maria Boiardo, yang menampilkan makhluk yang luar biasa cepat ini pada 1486 dalam L'Orlando Innamorato; pada 1532 Ariost mengambilnya ke dalam epos lanjutan Der rasende Roland. Artikel en justru menyebut makhluk ini ciptaan Ludovico Ariosto dalam Orlando Furioso pada awal abad ke-16, dengan bagian depan elang dan bagian belakang kuda.",
      "en": "The hippogryph (from Italian ippogrifo, combining hippos, \"horse\", and Italian grifo, \"griffin\") is a fabulous hybrid that is half griffin and half horse. According to the de article, the term was invented by Matteo Maria Boiardo, who featured this astonishingly fast creature in 1486 in L'Orlando Innamorato; in 1532 Ariost took it into his sequel epic Der rasende Roland. The en article instead credits Ludovico Ariosto's Orlando Furioso, early in the 16th century, picturing an eagle in front and a horse behind.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pferdegreif-c01",
        "pferdegreif-c02",
        "pferdegreif-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gagasan makhluk ini berasal dari ungkapan terpelajar bahwa persilangan griffin dan kuda adalah hal yang mustahil; Boiardo kemungkinan terilhami Eclogae karya Vergil, yang memakai perkawinan semacam itu sebagai kiasan pernikahan yang absurd. Dalam syair Ariosto, hippogryph lahir dari kuda betina dan griffin, sangat cepat, dan dapat terbang mengelilingi dunia hingga ke Bulan. Roger (Ruggiero) menyelamatkan putri Angelica dari monster laut dengan menungganginya, dan Astolfo terbang ke Bulan untuk mencari akal Roland yang hilang.",
      "en": "The idea comes from a learned saying that crossing a griffin with a horse is impossible; Boiardo was probably inspired by Vergil's Eclogae, which use such a pairing as a metaphor for an absurd marriage. In Ariosto's poem the hippogryph is born of a mare and a griffin, is extremely fast, and can fly around the world and to the Moon. Roger (Ruggiero) rescues princess Angelica from a sea monster riding it, and Astolfo flies to the Moon to find Roland's lost wits.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pferdegreif-c03",
        "pferdegreif-c04",
        "pferdegreif-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah-kisah kemudian, misalnya Oberon karya Christoph Martin Wieland, hippogryph sering menjadi sinonim Pegasus. Ia juga muncul dalam fantasi modern seperti Dungeons & Dragons dan seri Harry Potter, dengan hippogreif Seidenschnabel yang berperan penting dalam buku ketiga. Dalam heraldik, motif ini dikenal sebagai Pferdegreif.",
      "en": "In later tales, such as Christoph Martin Wieland's Oberon, the hippogryph often stands for Pegasus. It also appears in modern fantasy such as Dungeons & Dragons and the Harry Potter series, where the hippogreif Seidenschnabel plays a key role in the third book. In heraldry the motif is known as the Pferdegreif.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pferdegreif-c07",
        "pferdegreif-c08",
        "pferdegreif-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "pferdegreif-c01",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "Der Hippogryph (re-gräzisierte Form des italienischen ippogrifo, Zusammensetzung aus altgriechisch ἵππος hippos „Pferd“ und italienisch grifo „Greif“, dies aus lateinisch gryphus, dies wiederum aus altgriechisch γρύψ gryps) ist ein Fabelwesen im Sinne eines Mischwesens, das zur Hälfte Greif und zur anderen Hälfte Pferd ist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hippogryph, dari ippogrifo (hippos, kuda/Pferd, dan grifo, griffin/Greif), adalah makhluk dongeng campuran (Mischwesen), separuh griffin dan separuh kuda.",
        "en": "The hippogryph, from ippogrifo (hippos, horse/Pferd, and grifo, griffin/Greif), is a fabulous hybrid (Mischwesen), half griffin and half horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c02",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "Der aus dem Griechischen gebildete Begriff ist eine Erfindung von Matteo Maria Boiardo, der das unglaublich schnell rasende Fabelwesen 1486 in seinem Roman „Der verliebte Roland“ (L’Orlando Innamorato) auftreten ließ. 1532 übernahm Ariost den „Ross-Greif“ in sein Fortsetzungs-Epos „Der rasende Roland“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah ini ciptaan (Erfindung) Matteo Maria Boiardo dalam L'Orlando Innamorato (1486); pada 1532 Ariost mengambil \"Ross-Greif\" dalam Der rasende Roland.",
        "en": "The term is an invention (Erfindung) of Matteo Maria Boiardo in L'Orlando Innamorato (1486); in 1532 Ariost took the \"Ross-Greif\" into Der rasende Roland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c03",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "Die Idee zu dem Mischwesen basiert auf einer gelehrten Redewendung, wonach eine Kreuzung zwischen einem Greif und einem Pferd ein Ding der Unmöglichkeit sei. Vermutlich wurde Boiardo von einer Stelle in den Eclogae des Vergil inspiriert",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gagasan (Idee) makhluk ini berasal dari ungkapan (Redewendung) bahwa persilangan griffin (Greif) dan kuda (Pferd) mustahil; Boiardo mungkin terilhami Eclogae karya Vergil.",
        "en": "The idea (Idee) rests on a saying (Redewendung) that crossing a griffin (Greif) and a horse (Pferd) is impossible; Boiardo was probably inspired by Vergil's Eclogae."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c04",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "Roger (Ruggiero) rettet mit seiner Hilfe die schöne Prinzessin Angelica vor einem Meeresungeheuer, und der englische Prinz Astolfo fliegt auf dem Hippogryphen sogar zum Mond, um dort Rolands verlorenen Verstand wiederzufinden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roger (Ruggiero) menyelamatkan putri (Prinzessin) Angelica dari monster laut (Meeresungeheuer), dan Astolfo terbang ke Bulan (Mond) untuk mencari akal Roland.",
        "en": "Roger (Ruggiero) rescues princess (Prinzessin) Angelica from a sea monster (Meeresungeheuer), and Astolfo flies to the Moon (Mond) to find Roland's wits."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c05",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s2",
      "quote": "The hippogriff (Italian: ippogrifo ) or hippogryph is a legendary creature with the front half of an eagle and the hind half of a horse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hippogriff (Italia: ippogrifo, Italian) adalah makhluk legenda berbagian depan elang dan belakang kuda.",
        "en": "The hippogriff (Italian: ippogrifo) is a legendary creature with an eagle's front half and a horse's hind half."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c06",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s2",
      "quote": "It was invented at the beginning of the 16th century by Ludovico Ariosto in his Orlando Furioso. Within the poem, the hippogriff is a steed born of a mare and a griffin—something considered impossible. It is extremely fast and is presented as being able to fly around the world and to the Moon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk ini diciptakan Ludovico Ariosto dalam Orlando Furioso pada awal abad ke-16; lahir dari kuda betina dan griffin, sangat cepat, terbang mengelilingi dunia dan ke Bulan (Moon).",
        "en": "It was invented by Ludovico Ariosto in Orlando Furioso early in the 16th century; born of a mare and a griffin, extremely fast, able to fly around the world and to the Moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c07",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "In späteren Erzählungen, z. B. bei dem deutschen Dichter Christoph Martin Wieland (Oberon), taucht der Hippogryph oft als Synonym für Pegasus auf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah kemudian, misalnya Oberon karya Christoph Martin Wieland, hippogryph sering menjadi sinonim (Synonym) Pegasus.",
        "en": "In later tales, e.g. Christoph Martin Wieland's Oberon, the hippogryph is often a synonym (Synonym) for Pegasus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c08",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "In der deutschen Übersetzung der Harry-Potter-Romane von Joanne K. Rowling wird die Form „Hippogreif“ verwendet, die aber den in anderen Sprachen üblichen Bezeichnungen näher kommt. Im Englischen werden sowohl hippogriff (so bei Rowling) als auch hippogryph verwendet. Der Hippogreif Seidenschnabel spielt eine Schlüsselrolle im dritten Band",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Jerman (deutschen) novel Harry Potter karya Joanne K. Rowling memakai bentuk Hippogreif; Seidenschnabel adalah hippogreif dalam buku ketiga.",
        "en": "The German (deutschen) translation of Joanne K. Rowling's Harry Potter novels uses the form Hippogreif; Seidenschnabel is a hippogreif in the third book."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c09",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "In der Heraldik ist das Motiv unter der Bezeichnung Pferdegreif bekannt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam heraldik (Heraldik) motif ini dikenal sebagai Pferdegreif.",
        "en": "In heraldry (Heraldik) the motif is known as the Pferdegreif."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c10",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s2",
      "quote": "For him a filly to griffin bore; Hight hippogryph. In wings and beak and crest, Formed like his sire, as in the feet before; But like the mare, his dam, in all the rest. Such on Riphaean hills, though rarely found,",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Syair Orlando furioso: anak kuda betina dari griffin, bernama hippogryph, bersayap dan berparuh seperti ayahnya, selebihnya seperti induknya; jarang ditemukan di pegunungan Riphaean (Riphaean hills).",
        "en": "Orlando furioso verse: a filly bore him to a griffin, hight hippogryph, with wings and beak like his sire, the rest like the mare; rarely found on the Riphaean hills."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c11",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s1",
      "quote": "Gelegentlich taucht der Hippogryph in der modernen Fantasy auf, so unter anderem in Eric Rücker Eddisons Der Wurm Ouroboros (1922) und im Rollenspiel Dungeons & Dragons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hippogryph kadang muncul dalam fantasi modern (modernen Fantasy), antara lain dalam Der Wurm Ouroboros karya Eric Rücker Eddison (1922) dan permainan peran Dungeons & Dragons.",
        "en": "The hippogryph sometimes appears in modern fantasy (modernen Fantasy), including Eric Rücker Eddison's Der Wurm Ouroboros (1922) and the role-playing game Dungeons & Dragons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pferdegreif-c12",
      "source_id": "pferdegreif-s2",
      "quote": "The word hippogriff was adopted into English shortly before 1615; prior to this, John Harington's 1591 translation of Orlando furioso called the creature a \"Griffith horse.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata hippogriff masuk ke bahasa Inggris (English) menjelang 1615; terjemahan John Harington (1591) menyebutnya \"Griffith horse\".",
        "en": "The word hippogriff entered English shortly before 1615; John Harington's 1591 translation called it a \"Griffith horse\"."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Phinaert adalah raksasa (géant) dalam legenda pendirian kota Lille, penguasa setempat di Flandria yang dibunuh oleh Lydéric dalam duel.",
    "en": "Phinaert is a giant (géant) in the founding legend of Lille, a local lord in Flanders slain by Lydéric in a duel.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lydéric dan Phinaert adalah dua tokoh dalam legenda pendirian kota Lille di Flandria Prancis. Menurut legenda, sekitar tahun 620 pangeran Dijon, Salvaert, dan istrinya yang sedang hamil, Ermengaert, melewati Flandria dan masuk ke perangkap penguasa setempat, raksasa (géant) Phinaert. Phinaert membunuh sang pangeran dan anak buahnya, sedangkan Ermengaert lari ke seorang pertapa di hutan dan melahirkan seorang putra yang kemudian dinamai Lydéric.",
      "en": "Lydéric and Phinaert are two figures of the founding legend of Lille in French Flanders. According to the legend, around 620 the prince of Dijon, Salvaert, and his pregnant wife Ermengaert passed through Flanders and fell into an ambush laid by the local lord, the giant (géant) Phinaert. Phinaert had the prince and his men killed, while Ermengaert fled to a hermit in the forest and bore a son, later named Lydéric.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "phinaert-c01",
        "phinaert-c02",
        "phinaert-c07"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Setelah dewasa, Lydéric mengetahui asal-usulnya dan menemukan Phinaert di istana raja Dagobert I di Soissons. Versi lama kisah ini terdapat dalam Annales de Flandre.",
      "en": "As an adult, Lydéric learned of his origins and found Phinaert at the court of King Dagobert I in Soissons.       "quote": "Lors de leur trajet dans les Flandres, ils tombent dans une embuscade tendue par le seigneur local, le géant Phinaert. Celui-ci fait assassiner le prince et ses hommes, tandis qu'Ermengaert s'enfuit et trouve refuge auprès d'un ermite dans la forêt.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Sekitar 620 pangeran Dijon, Salvaert, dan istrinya yang hamil, Ermengaert, melewati Flandria (Flanders) dan masuk perangkap raksasa Phinaert.",
        "en": "Around 620 the prince of Dijon, Salvaert, and his pregnant wife Ermengaert passed through Flanders and fell into a trap of the giant Phinaert."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "phinaert-c08",
      "source_id": "phinaert-s3",
      "quote": "Phinaert, ch'est un d'ches gayants d'Lille.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Phinaert adalah salah satu gayant (raksasa pawai) kota Lille (d'Lille).",
        "en": "Phinaert is one of the gayants (processional giants) of Lille (d'Lille)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "phinaert-c09",
      "source_id": "phinaert-s1",
      "quote": "L’écrivain écrit ce roman, qui parait en 1841, à la demande de Samuel-Henri Berthoud",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alexandre Dumas menulis novel (roman) Aventures de Lydéric yang terbit 1841 atas permintaan Samuel-Henri Berthoud.",
        "en": "Alexandre Dumas wrote the novel (roman) Aventures de Lydéric, published in 1841 at the request of Samuel-Henri Berthoud."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "phinaert-c10",
      "source_id": "phinaert-s1",
      "quote": "Devenu adulte, Lydéric apprend la vérité sur son passé et retrouve la trace de Phinaert à la cour du roi Dagobert Ier à Soissons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah dewasa (adulte), Lydéric mengetahui masa lalunya dan menemukan Phinaert di istana raja (roi) Dagobert I di Soissons.",
        "en": "As an adult (adulte), Lydéric learned the truth and found Phinaert at the court of King (roi) Dagobert I in Soissons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "phinaert-c11",
      "source_id": "phinaert-s1",
      "quote": "Émile Pierre Ratez, compositeur, altiste et directeur du conservatoire de Lille a consacré à cette légende un opéra en 3 actes et 4 tableaux",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Émile Pierre Ratez, komponis (compositeur) dan direktur konservatori Lille, membuat opera tentang legenda (légende) ini.",
        "en": "Émile Pierre Ratez, composer (compositeur) and director of the Lille conservatory, devoted an opera to this legend (légende)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "phinaert-c12",
      "source_id": "phinaert-s1",
      "quote": "Alexandre Dumas, Aventures de Lydéric Grand Forestier de Flandre, (ISBN 2-85984-023-0) L’écrivain écrit ce roman, qui parait en 1841",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alexandre Dumas menulis Aventures de Lydéric Grand Forestier de Flandre, novel (roman) yang terbit 1841.",
        "en": "Alexandre Dumas wrote Aventures de Lydéric Grand Forestier de Flandre, a novel (roman) published in 1841."
      }
    }
  ],
