  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c01",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "It was known among the former Germans of Bohemia and Silesia, now part of the Czech Republic and Poland respectively, particularly in the former Iglau language island of Bohemia. The Uhaml is an airy sprite, a ghost, or possibly some kind of demonic bird. Nothing is known about its appearance other than it having horse feet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uhaml dikenal di Bohemia dan Silesia (kini Republik Ceko dan Polandia; Czech Republic, Poland), terutama Iglau; roh udara, hantu, atau burung iblis berkaki kuda.",
        "en": "The Uhaml is a spirit of German folktales in Bohemia and Silesia (now Czech Republic and Poland), especially Iglau; an airy sprite, ghost or demonic bird with horse feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c02",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "At night, the Uhaml's call can be heard from the air: \"Uhuhu!\" People mocking it by imitating its call will be chased by the Uhaml until they are safe from it under the eaves.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam hari seruan Uhaml \"Uhuhu!\" terdengar dari udara; yang menirunya dikejar hingga aman di bawah atap.",
        "en": "At night the Uhaml's call \"Uhuhu!\" is heard from the air; mockers who imitate it are chased until safe under the eaves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c03",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "In the forest, \"she\" (for the Uhaml is apparently imagined as female in this case only) might also aufhocken, i.e. leap on a passerby's back and let itself be carried, jumping down from \"her\" victim only upon reaching the forest border.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di hutan, Uhaml (di sini perempuan) dapat aufhocken, melompat ke punggung orang dan turun di tepi hutan.",
        "en": "In the forest the Uhaml (here female) may aufhocken, leaping on a passerby's back until the forest border."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c04",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "To ward off the Uhaml's evildoing, three chips of wood and a loaf of bread will be placed in the parlor. The Uhaml will enter the parlor, cut the bread in two halves and take one of them with it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menangkal Uhaml, tiga serpih kayu dan roti diletakkan di ruang tamu; Uhaml membelah roti dan membawa separuhnya.",
        "en": "To ward off the Uhaml, three wood chips and a loaf are placed in the parlour; the Uhaml halves the bread and takes one half."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c05",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "The Uhaml and other legendary creatures might be based on the Eurasian eagle-owl.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uhaml dan makhluk legenda lain mungkin berasal dari burung hantu besar Eurasia (Eurasian eagle-owl).",
        "en": "The Uhaml and other legendary creatures might be based on the Eurasian eagle-owl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c06",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "The name Uhaml can be explained as a Silesian dialectal form of Standard German \"unheimlich\", meaning \"eerie\" or \"uncanny\", or can more likely be connected to the Eurasian eagle-owl, called \"Uhu\" in German with regard to its call.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Uhaml mungkin bentuk dialek Silesia (Silesian) dari unheimlich (seram), atau lebih mungkin dari Uhu, burung hantu besar Eurasia (Eurasian eagle-owl).",
        "en": "The name Uhaml may be a Silesian form of unheimlich (eerie), or more likely from Uhu, the Eurasian eagle-owl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c07",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "In Lower Austria, there is the Udl, a demonic kind of bird dwelling in the woods covering the slopes of the castle mountain (Schlossberg) in Mödling. Its hoarse, raucous call can be heard relentlessly at night. The call of the Udl is a true omen for war or other disasters.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Lower Austria (Austria Hilir) ada Udl, burung iblis di lereng Schlossberg di Mödling yang seruannya pertanda perang atau bencana.",
        "en": "In Lower Austria there is the Udl, a demonic bird on the Schlossberg slopes at Mödling whose call is an omen of war or disaster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c08",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "In the Steigerwald in Franconia, there is a stone cross on the path from Ebrach to Michelau. There, the Uhumännle is haunting, a ghost known for its habit of shouting \"uhu\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Steigerwald, Franconia, Uhumännle menghantui salib batu di jalan Ebrach ke Michelau sambil berseru \"uhu\".",
        "en": "In the Steigerwald in Franconia the Uhumännle haunts a stone cross on the Ebrach–Michelau path, shouting \"uhu\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c09",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "Those who mock it by imitating its \"uhu\" will get slapped by an invisible hand.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang yang meniru seruan \"uhu\" Uhumännle ditampar tangan tak kasatmata.",
        "en": "Those who imitate the Uhumännle's \"uhu\" are slapped by an invisible hand."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uhaml-c10",
      "source_id": "uhaml-s1",
      "quote": "The Uhaml (German: das Uhaml with neuter article; German: [ˈuːˌhaml̩] ) is a spirit from German folktales.",
      "locator": null,

        "en": "The Uhaml (das Uhaml, neuter) is a spirit of German folktales."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jätten Vist adalah raksasa (jätte) dalam legenda yang menciptakan pulau Visingsö di danau Vättern.",
    "en": "Vist the giant (jätte) is a figure of legend who created the island of Visingsö in Lake Vättern.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vist-the-giant-c01",
      "vist-the-giant-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, Jätten Vist adalah raksasa (jätte) yang menciptakan Visingsö. Ia dan istrinya pulang dari pesta di Västergötland dan hendak melangkahi danau Vättern kembali ke Småland. Vist mengambil segumpal besar tanah berumput (tuva) dan melemparkannya ke danau sebagai pijakan; gumpalan itu kini menjadi pulau Visingsö, dan lubang bekas ia mengambilnya menjadi danau Landsjön. Menurut artikel tentang Visingsö, pijakan itu dibuat untuk istrinya, sementara Vist sendiri hanya perlu satu langkah untuk menyeberangi danau.",
      "en": "According to legend, Jätten Vist is a giant (jätte) who created Visingsö. He and his wife had been at a feast in Västergötland and were about to step over Lake Vättern back to Småland. Vist took a large clump of turf (tuva) and threw it into the lake as a stepping stone; that clump is now the island of Visingsö, and the hole he took it from became the lake Landsjön. According to the articles on Visingsö, the stepping stone was for his wife, while Vist himself needed only one stride to cross the lake.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vist-the-giant-c01",
        "vist-the-giant-c03",
        "vist-the-giant-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sejak 1969 patung Calle Örnemark yang menggambarkan Vist dengan gumpalan tanahnya berdiri di Strandvägen di Huskvarna; patung itu dipugar beberapa bulan sebelum sang seniman meninggal pada musim panas 2015. Vist juga disebut dalam lagu pertama, Tror jag hittar hem, dari album Eldtuppen karya penyanyi Lars Winnerbäck.",
      "en": "Since 1969 a sculpture by Calle Örnemark showing Vist with his clump of turf has stood on Strandvägen in Huskvarna; it was restored a few months before the artist died in summer 2015. Vist is also mentioned in the first track, Tror jag hittar hem, on singer Lars Winnerbäck's album Eldtuppen.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vist-the-giant-c01",
      "source_id": "vist-the-giant-s1",
      "quote": "Jätten Vist är enligt legenden en jätte som skapade Visingsö, då han och hans fru hade varit på fest i Västergötland och skulle kliva över Vättern tillbaka till Småland. Han tog då en stor tuva och kastade ut den i sjön för att kliva på, och den tuvan återfinns numera som Visingsö. Tuvan han tog blev då sjön Landsjön.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda (legenden), Jätten Vist adalah raksasa (jätte) pencipta Visingsö: pulang dari pesta (fest) di Västergötland ke Småland, ia melempar gumpalan tanah (tuva) ke Vättern sebagai pijakan; bekasnya menjadi Landsjön.",
        "en": "According to legend (legenden), Jätten Vist is a giant (jätte) who made Visingsö: returning from a feast (fest) in Västergötland to Småland, he threw a turf (tuva) into Vättern to step on; the hole became Landsjön."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vist-the-giant-c02",
      "source_id": "vist-the-giant-s1",
      "quote": "Sedan 1969 finns en skulptur av Calle Örnemark, föreställande jätten Vist med sin tuva, vid Strandvägen i Huskvarna. Den restaurerades ett par månader före konstnärens död sommaren 2015.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak 1969 patung (skulptur) karya Calle Örnemark tentang Vist dan tuva-nya berdiri di Strandvägen, Huskvarna; dipugar (restaurerades) sebelum sang seniman wafat 2015.",
        "en": "Since 1969 a sculpture (skulptur) by Calle Örnemark of Vist with his tuva stands on Strandvägen in Huskvarna; restored (restaurerades) before the artist's death in 2015."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vist-the-giant-c03",
      "source_id": "vist-the-giant-s2",
      "quote": "According to legend, a giant named Vist created Visingsö by throwing a lump of soil into the lake so that his wife could use it to step over the lake.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, raksasa bernama Vist menciptakan Visingsö dengan melempar segumpal tanah ke danau sebagai pijakan istrinya.",
        "en": "By legend a giant named Vist created Visingsö by throwing a lump of soil into the lake for his wife to step on."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vist-the-giant-c04",
      "source_id": "vist-the-giant-s3",
      "quote": "Enligt legenden skapades ön av jätten Vist när han kastade ut en tuva i sjön för att hans fru skulle ha något att kliva på när hon tog sig över Vättern. Vist själv behövde bara ett steg för att komma över sjön.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda (legenden), pulau (ön) itu diciptakan raksasa (jätten) Vist sebagai pijakan istrinya (fru) di Vättern; Vist sendiri hanya perlu satu langkah (ett steg).",
        "en": "By legend (legenden) the island (ön) was made by the giant (jätten) Vist as a step for his wife (fru) over Vättern; Vist himself needed only one step (ett steg)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vist-the-giant-c05",
      "source_id": "vist-the-giant-s1",
      "quote": "Jätten Vist omnämns i första spåret Tror jag hittar hem på sångaren Lars Winnerbäcks skiva Eldtuppen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jätten Vist disebut dalam lagu pertama (första spåret) Tror jag hittar hem di album (skiva) Eldtuppen karya penyanyi (sångaren) Lars Winnerbäck.",
        "en": "Jätten Vist is mentioned in the first track (första spåret) Tror jag hittar hem on singer (sångaren) Lars Winnerbäck's album (skiva) Eldtuppen."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vita Karin adalah hantu (spöke) di kastel Snogeholm menurut kepercayaan rakyat: arwah pelayan muda yang dibunuh secara keji pada akhir 1700-an.",
    "en": "Vita Karin is a ghost (spöke) of Snogeholm Castle according to folk belief: the spirit of a young servant cruelly killed in the late 1700s.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vita-karin-c01",
      "vita-karin-c02",
      "vita-karin-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat (folktro), Vita Karin adalah hantu di kastel Snogeholm (Snogeholms slott). Semasa hidup ia disebut \"Lilla Karin\" dan bekerja di ruang makan kastel pada akhir 1700-an; ia sering dimarahi karena mencelupkan jari ke pai saat membawanya. Seorang bangsawan menaruh hati padanya, sehingga orang lain ingin mempermainkannya: mereka mengirim surat lamaran palsu dan memintanya datang ke sebuah gua di luar kastel.",
      "en": "In folk belief (folktro), Vita Karin is a ghost of Snogeholm Castle (Snogeholms slott). In life she was called \"Lilla Karin\" and worked in the castle's dining room in the late 1700s; she was often scolded for putting her fingers in the pies she carried. A nobleman took a fancy to her, which made others want to play a trick on her: they sent her a false letter of proposal and asked her to come to a cave outside the castle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vita-karin-c01",
        "vita-karin-c02",
        "vita-karin-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Karin menjahit gaun pengantin dari tirainya dan pergi ke tempat pertemuan, tetapi yang menunggunya adalah lima lelaki yang menganiayanya. Ia dibawa kembali ke kastel dan pagi berikutnya ditemukan mati, pucat pasi, dalam gaun putih. Arwahnya biasa menampakkan diri sebagai cahaya putih atau kilatan putih. Kastel Snogeholm yang sekarang dibangun pada akhir 1860-an setelah bangunan asli dari abad ke-17 terbakar, dan sebuah kereta pågatåg kini bernama \"Vita Karin\".",
      "en": "Karin sewed a wedding dress from her curtains and went to the meeting place, but five men were waiting for her and assaulted her. She was carried back to the castle and found the next morning dead, deathly pale, in a white dress. Her spirit usually appears as a white glow or a white flash. The present Snogeholm Castle was built in the late 1860s after the original 17th-century building burned down, and a pågatåg train now bears the name \"Vita Karin\".",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vita-karin-c04",
        "vita-karin-c05",
        "vita-karin-c06",
        "vita-karin-c07"
      ]

      "source_id": "vita-karin-s1",
      "quote": "En adelsman hade ett gott öga till henne, vilket fick andra att vilja spela henne ett spratt. De skickade ett falskt friarbrev till Karin, och bad henne komma till en grotta utanför slottet.",
      "locator": null,

    {
      "id": "vita-karin-c04",
      "source_id": "vita-karin-s1",
      "quote": "Karin sydde en brudklänning av sina gardiner och begav sig till möteplatsen. Istället för adelsmannen väntade fem män på henne, som våldförde sig på henne och misshandlade henne. Hon bars tillbaks till slottet, där man fann henne nästa morgon, död, likblek och i en vit klänning.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karin menjahit gaun pengantin (brudklänning) dari tirai (gardiner); lima lelaki (fem män) menganiayanya, dan pagi berikutnya ia ditemukan mati (död), pucat, dalam gaun putih (vit klänning).",

      ]
    }
  ],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Waterreus adalah tokoh legenda desa nelayan Scheveningen: lelaki atau roh terkutuk yang hidup di laut, lalu menikahi seorang gadis setempat dan menjadi leluhur keluarga Waterreus.",
    "en": "The waterreus is a figure of legend from the fishing village of Scheveningen: a cursed man or spirit living in the sea who married a local girl and became ancestor of the Waterreus family.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "waterreus-c01",
      "waterreus-c02",
      "waterreus-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Waterreus adalah tokoh dari sebuah legenda desa nelayan Scheveningen di Holland Selatan (Zuid-Hollandse), dan juga nama sebuah keluarga Scheveningen yang terkait dengan legenda itu. Waterreus adalah seorang lelaki atau roh (geest) yang dulu hidup di laut di depan Scheveningen. Ia terkutuk untuk selamanya hidup di antara ombak, dan hanya seorang gadis yang pada malam hari membuat tanda salib di dahinya dengan jari telunjuk yang dapat menyelamatkannya.",
      "en": "The waterreus is a figure from a legend of the South Holland (Zuid-Hollandse) fishing village of Scheveningen, and also the name of a Scheveningen family tied to the legend. The waterreus was a man or spirit (geest) who once lived in the sea off Scheveningen. He was cursed to live forever in the waves, and only a girl who at night made a cross on his forehead with her forefinger could save him.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "waterreus-c01",
        "waterreus-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seorang janda miskin punya putri yang sangat cantik, yang setiap hari disuruh menangkap ikan agar tak kelaparan. Awalnya gadis itu tak mau berurusan dengan roh menyeramkan itu, tetapi lambat laun mereka saling jatuh cinta. Ketika banyaknya ikan yang digiring waterreus ke jalanya membuat warga desa iri, ia mengaku punya sekutu di laut; setelah warga yang tak percaya melihatnya sendiri, waterreus naik ke darat, mereka menikah, dan keturunan mereka yang banyak sejak itu bernama Waterreus.",
      "en": "A penniless widow had a beautiful daughter who was sent out fishing every day so they would not starve. At first the girl wanted nothing to do with the eerie spirit, but in time they fell in love. When the amount of fish the waterreus drove into her nets made the villagers jealous, she said she had an ally in the sea; once the doubting villagers saw it for themselves, he came ashore, they married, and their many descendants have since been called Waterreus.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "waterreus-c03",
        "waterreus-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Keluarga Waterreus di Scheveningen tetap beragama Katolik Roma, tidak pernah memakai pakaian adat setempat, dan sebagai pengangkut barang mampu untuk tidak menjadi Protestan pada abad ke-16 seperti seluruh desa; tanda salib di dahi dalam legenda itu adalah simbol Katolik.",
      "en": "The Waterreus family of Scheveningen remained Roman Catholic, never wore the local costume, and as carters could afford not to turn Protestant in the 16th century like the rest of the village; the cross on the forehead in the legend is a Catholic symbol.",

        "waterreus-c05"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "waterreus-c01",
      "source_id": "waterreus-s1",
      "quote": "De waterreus is een personage uit een legende uit het Zuid-Hollandse vissersdorp Scheveningen. Daarnaast is het de naam van een familie uit Scheveningen, die aan deze legende verbonden is.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Waterreus adalah tokoh (personage) legenda (legende) desa nelayan (vissersdorp) Scheveningen di Holland Selatan (Zuid-Hollandse), juga nama keluarga (familie) yang terkait legenda itu.",
        "en": "The waterreus is a figure (personage) of a legend (legende) of the South Holland (Zuid-Hollandse) fishing village (vissersdorp) of Scheveningen, also the name of a family (familie) tied to it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "waterreus-c02",
      "source_id": "waterreus-s1",
      "quote": "De waterreus is een manspersoon of geest die vroeger voor Scheveningen in de zee leefde. Hij was vervloekt en gedoemd voor altijd in de golven te leven. Alleen een meisje dat 's nachts met haar wijsvinger een kruis op zijn voorhoofd zou maken, kon hem redden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Waterreus adalah lelaki atau roh (geest) yang hidup di laut (zee), terkutuk (vervloekt) hidup di ombak (golven); hanya gadis (meisje) yang membuat salib (kruis) di dahinya (voorhoofd) dapat menyelamatkannya.",
        "en": "The waterreus was a man or spirit (geest) living in the sea (zee), cursed (vervloekt) to live in the waves (golven); only a girl (meisje) making a cross (kruis) on his forehead (voorhoofd) could save him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "waterreus-c03",
      "source_id": "waterreus-s1",
      "quote": "Een straatarme weduwe had een beeldschone dochter, die om niet te verhongeren, elke dag uit vissen werd gestuurd. Het meisje moest in het begin niets hebben van de enge geest, maar na verloop van tijd werden ze toch op elkaar verliefd.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Janda miskin (weduwe) punya putri (dochter) cantik yang disuruh menangkap ikan (vissen); ia mula-mula takut pada roh (geest) itu, tetapi lalu mereka jatuh cinta (verliefd).",
        "en": "A poor widow (weduwe) had a beautiful daughter (dochter) sent fishing (vissen); at first she shunned the spirit (geest), but they fell in love (verliefd)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "waterreus-c04",
      "source_id": "waterreus-s1",
      "quote": "Toen de hoeveelheid vis die de waterreus steeds in haar netten dreef, de jaloezie van de andere dorpsgenoten opwekte, vertelde ze dat ze een bondgenoot in de zee had. Toen de ongelovige dorpsgenoten het zelf uiteindelijk hadden gezien, kwam hij aan land waarna ze trouwden en hun talrijk nageslacht heet sindsdien waterreus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikan (vis) yang digiring waterreus ke jalanya (netten) membuat warga iri (jaloezie); ia naik ke darat (aan land), mereka menikah (trouwden), dan keturunan (nageslacht) mereka bernama waterreus.",
        "en": "The fish (vis) the waterreus drove into her nets (netten) roused jealousy (jaloezie); he came ashore (aan land), they married (trouwden), and their descendants (nageslacht) are called waterreus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "waterreus-c05",
      "source_id": "waterreus-s1",
      "quote": "Opvallend is dat de Scheveningse familie Waterreus altijd rooms-katholiek is gebleven, nooit de lokale klederdracht heeft gedragen én het zich als voerlui konden veroorloven om niet, zoals de rest van het dorp, in de 16e eeuw protestant te worden. Het kruisje maken op het voorhoofd is, zoals in de legende, een katholiek symbool.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keluarga (familie) Waterreus tetap Katolik Roma (rooms-katholiek), tak memakai pakaian adat (klederdracht), tak menjadi Protestan (protestant) pada abad ke-16; salib di dahi adalah simbol Katolik (katholiek symbool).",
        "en": "The Waterreus family (familie) stayed Roman Catholic (rooms-katholiek), never wore local costume (klederdracht), did not turn Protestant (protestant) in the 16th century; the forehead cross is a Catholic symbol (katholiek symbool)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dikhash kochi (დიხაშ კოჩი, \"manusia tanah\") adalah kurcaci jahat (ავსული ჯუჯა) dalam mitologi Megrelia, sebesar ჭინკა dan berwajah manusia, yang berjalan merangkak.",
    "en": "Dikhash kochi (დიხაშ კოჩი, \"earth man\") is an evil dwarf (ავსული ჯუჯა) of Megrelian mythology, the size of a ჭინკა and human-faced, that moves on all fours.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wd-q136444079-c01",
      "wd-q136444079-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dikhash kochi (დიხაშ კოჩი; berarti \"manusia tanah\") adalah kurcaci jahat dalam mitologi Megrelia. Tubuhnya sebesar ჭინკა (chinka, sejenis jin kecil) dan, seperti chinka, berwajah manusia. Ia dinamai demikian karena tidak dapat berjalan tegak, melainkan bergerak dengan empat kaki.",
      "en": "Dikhash kochi (დიხაშ კოჩი; meaning \"earth man\") is an evil dwarf of Megrelian mythology. It is the size of a ჭინკა (chinka, a small imp) and, like a chinka, has a human face. It bears this name because it cannot walk upright but moves on all fours.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wd-q136444079-c01",
        "wd-q136444079-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia muncul di desa-desa yang banyak rumahnya dan sering bersembunyi di halaman rumah; kadang orang juga bertemu dengannya di jalan desa. Karena bergerak lambat, ia jarang menangkap korbannya dan orang mudah lolos darinya. Ia menyusup di antara kaki pejalan kaki untuk menjatuhkannya; bila bertemu penunggang kuda, ia mencengkeram kekang sehingga kuda tak dapat melangkah. Ada pula kejadian ketika dikhash kochi membuat orang menjadi gila.",
      "en": "It appears in villages with many houses and often hides in house yards; sometimes people also meet it on village roads. Because it moves slowly it rarely catches its victim, and people escape it easily. It slips between the legs of walkers to trip them; if it meets a rider, it grabs the bridle so the horse cannot take a step. There have also been cases where the dikhash kochi drove a person mad.",

        "wd-q136444079-c03",
        "wd-q136444079-c04",
        "wd-q136444079-c05"
      ]
    }

      "locator": null,

        "en": "დიხაშ კოჩი („მიწის კაცი“, earth man) is an evil dwarf (ავსული ჯუჯა) of Megrelian mythology (მეგრულ მითოლოგიაში)."

    {
      "id": "wd-q136444079-c02",
      "source_id": "wd-q136444079-s1",
      "quote": "ტანით ჭინკის ოდენაა, და ჭინკის მსგავსად ადამიანის სახისაა. ეს სახელი იმიტომ ჰქვია, რომ ვერტიკალურად ვერ დადის, და ოთხზე დგას და ისე მოძრაობს.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ukurannya sebesar ჭინკის (chinka), berwajah manusia (ადამიანის სახისაა), dan bergerak dengan empat kaki (ოთხზე დგას).",
        "en": "It is the size of a chinka (ჭინკის), human-faced (ადამიანის სახისაა), and moves standing on all fours (ოთხზე დგას)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wd-q136444079-c03",
      "source_id": "wd-q136444079-s1",
      "quote": "ჩნდება ხოლმე სოფლებში, სადაც სახლები მრავლადაა, და ხშირად სახლის ეზოებშიც იმალება ხოლმე. ზოგჯერ კაცს სოფლის გზებზეც ხვდება. რადგან ის ნელ-ნელა მოძრაობს, თავის მსხვერპლს იშვიათად იჭერს",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul di desa (სოფლებში) dan bersembunyi di halaman rumah (სახლის ეზოებშიც); karena lambat (ნელ-ნელა) ia jarang menangkap korbannya (მსხვერპლს).",
        "en": "It appears in villages (სოფლებში) and hides in house yards (სახლის ეზოებშიც); being slow (ნელ-ნელა) it rarely catches its victim (მსხვერპლს)."
      }

      "source_id": "wd-q136444079-s1",
      "quote": "ქვეითად მიმავალთ ფეხებშუა უძვრება და ცდილობს წააქციოს; თუ მხედარს შეხვდა, აღვირს წაავლებს ხელს და ცხენს ფეხის გადადგმის საშუალებას არ აძლევს.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Weiße Frauen (\"Perempuan Putih\") adalah roh mirip elf dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, makhluk cantik dan tersihir yang muncul di siang hari, menjaga harta atau menghantui kastel.",
    "en": "The Weiße Frauen (\"White Women\") are elf-like spirits of German folklore, beautiful enchanted beings who appear at noon and guard treasure or haunt castles.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "wei-e-frauen-c01",
      "wei-e-frauen-c02",
      "wei-e-frauen-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, Weiße Frauen (\"Perempuan Putih\") adalah roh mirip elf yang mungkin berasal dari paganisme Jermanik, dalam bentuk legenda elf cahaya (Ljósálfar). Mereka digambarkan sebagai makhluk cantik dan tersihir yang muncul pada tengah hari dan terlihat duduk di bawah sinar matahari sambil menyisir rambut atau mandi di sungai kecil. Mereka mungkin menjaga harta atau menghantui kastel, dan memohon manusia mematahkan sihir mereka, tetapi upaya itu selalu gagal. Mitologi ini setidaknya berasal dari Abad Pertengahan.",
      "en": "In German folklore, the Weiße Frauen (\"White Women\") are elf-like spirits, perhaps rooted in Germanic paganism through tales of the light elves (Ljósálfar). They are described as beautiful, enchanted beings who appear at noon and may be spotted in the sun combing their hair, or bathing in a stream. They may guard treasure or haunt castles, and they beg mortals to break their spell, but this always fails. The mythology goes back at least to the Middle Ages.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wei-e-frauen-c01",
        "wei-e-frauen-c02",
        "wei-e-frauen-c03",
        "wei-e-frauen-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Jacob Grimm menduga kaitan mereka dengan warna putih dan sinar matahari berasal dari elf cahaya Ljósálfar yang tinggal di Álfheim di bawah dewa Freyr; mereka juga mungkin mewakili kepercayaan lama tentang roh leluhur atau dewi kuno seperti Holda, Berhta, dan Ostara. Menurut Grimm, sihir yang menimpa mereka mungkin melambangkan larangan yang dijatuhkan Kekristenan atas dewa-dewa kepercayaan lama.",
      "en": "Jacob Grimm thought their link with white and sunlight came from the light elves (Ljósálfar) who lived in Álfheim under the god Freyr; they may also reflect older beliefs in ancestral spirits or ancient goddesses such as Holda, Berhta and Ostara. According to Grimm, the enchantment they suffer may symbolize the ban Christianity laid on the gods of the older faith.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wei-e-frauen-c05",
        "wei-e-frauen-c06",
        "wei-e-frauen-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka memiliki padanan di negeri tetangga: di Belanda disebut Witte Wieven, di Prancis Dames Blanches, dan padanan dalam bahasa Jerman rendah (Low German) adalah Witte Wiwer; di wilayah Alpen Austria dan Jerman Selatan mereka disebut Salige Frauen. Di Belanda, witte wieven adalah perempuan dalam legenda yang kadang baik, kadang jahat, yang sering dikaitkan dengan penyihir atau hantu dan diyakini tinggal di bukit kubur dan hunebed.",
      "en": "They have counterparts in neighbouring lands: in the Netherlands the Witte Wieven, in France the Dames Blanches, and their Low German equivalent is the Witte Wiwer; in the Alpine parts of Austria and the south of Germany they go by Salige Frauen. In the Netherlands the witte wieven are women of legend, sometimes kind and sometimes wicked, often linked with witches or ghosts and believed to live in burial mounds and hunebeds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "wei-e-frauen-c07",
        "wei-e-frauen-c08",
        "wei-e-frauen-c09",
        "wei-e-frauen-c10",
        "wei-e-frauen-c11"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c01",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "In German folklore, the Weiße Frauen (German: [ˈvaɪsə ˈfʁaʊən] , meaning White Women) are elf-like spirits which may derive from Germanic paganism in the form of legends of light elves (Old Norse: Ljósálfar).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman (German folklore), Weiße Frauen (White Women) adalah roh mirip elf dari paganisme Jermanik (Germanic), legenda elf cahaya (Ljósálfar).",
        "en": "In German folklore the Weiße Frauen (White Women) are elf-like spirits possibly from Germanic paganism, legends of light elves (Ljósálfar)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c02",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "They are described as beautiful and enchanted creatures who appear at noon and can be seen sitting in the sunshine brushing their hair or bathing in a brook.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka makhluk cantik dan tersihir yang muncul pada tengah hari, menyisir rambut di bawah matahari atau mandi di sungai kecil.",
        "en": "They are beautiful enchanted creatures appearing at noon, brushing their hair in the sunshine or bathing in a brook."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c03",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "They may be guarding treasure or haunting castles. They entreat mortals to break their spell, but this is always unsuccessful.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menjaga harta atau menghantui kastel, dan memohon manusia mematahkan sihirnya, tetapi selalu gagal.",
        "en": "They may guard treasure or haunt castles, and entreat mortals to break their spell, always unsuccessfully."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c04",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "The mythology dates back at least to the Middle Ages and was known in the present-day area of Germany.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitologi ini setidaknya dari Abad Pertengahan (Middle Ages) dan dikenal di wilayah Jerman (Germany) sekarang.",
        "en": "The mythology dates back at least to the Middle Ages and was known in present-day Germany."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c05",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "The association with the color white and their appearance in sunlight is thought by Jacob Grimm to stem from the original Old Norse and Teutonic mythology of alven (elves), specifically the bright Ljósálfar. These \"light elves\" lived in Álfheim (part of heaven) under the fertility god Freyr.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jacob Grimm mengaitkan warna putih dan sinar matahari dengan elf cahaya (Ljósálfar) yang tinggal di Álfheim di bawah dewa Freyr.",
        "en": "Jacob Grimm traced their whiteness and sunlight to the light elves (Ljósálfar) living in Álfheim under the god Freyr."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c06",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "According to Grimm's Teutonic Mythology and to the Mythology of All Races Series, the enchantment under which they suffer \"may be a symbol of the ban laid by Christianity on the divinities of the older faith.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Teutonic Mythology karya Grimm, sihir yang menimpa mereka mungkin lambang larangan Kekristenan (Christianity) atas dewa kepercayaan lama.",
        "en": "According to Grimm's Teutonic Mythology, their enchantment may symbolize the ban laid by Christianity on the older gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c07",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "The Weiße Frauen also have counterparts in both name and characterization in neighboring countries: In the Netherlands they are known as the Witte Wieven, and in France as the Dames Blanches. Their Low German equivalent are the Witte Wiwer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan Weiße Frauen: Witte Wieven di Belanda (Netherlands), Dames Blanches di Prancis (France), dan Witte Wiwer dalam Jerman Rendah (Low German).",
        "en": "Counterparts of the Weiße Frauen: the Witte Wieven in the Netherlands, the Dames Blanches in France, and the Low German Witte Wiwer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c08",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "In the Alpine regions of Austria and Southern Germany they are called Salige Frauen or just Salige (Salkweiber, Salaweiber).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di wilayah Alpen (Alpine) Austria dan Jerman Selatan (Southern Germany) mereka disebut Salige Frauen atau Salige.",
        "en": "In the Alpine regions of Austria and Southern Germany they are called Salige Frauen or Salige."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c09",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s2",
      "quote": "Het is de naam voor vrouwenfiguren die in sagen voorkomen en soms goedaardig, soms kwaadaardig zijn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Witte wieven adalah nama tokoh perempuan (vrouwenfiguren) dalam legenda (sagen) yang kadang baik (goedaardig), kadang jahat (kwaadaardig).",
        "en": "Witte wieven is the name for female figures (vrouwenfiguren) in legends (sagen), sometimes kind (goedaardig), sometimes wicked (kwaadaardig)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c10",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s2",
      "quote": "Witte wieven worden vaak in verband gebracht met heksen of spoken.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Witte wieven sering dikaitkan dengan penyihir (heksen) atau hantu (spoken).",
        "en": "Witte wieven are often linked with witches (heksen) or ghosts (spoken)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c11",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s2",
      "quote": "Megalithische monumenten als ganggraven, grafheuvels en hunebedden werden door sommigen als hun woonsteden gezien.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Monumen megalitik (Megalithische monumenten) seperti bukit kubur (grafheuvels) dan hunebed (hunebedden) dianggap tempat tinggal (woonsteden) witte wieven.",
        "en": "Megalithic monuments (Megalithische monumenten) such as burial mounds (grafheuvels) and hunebeds (hunebedden) were seen as the witte wieven's homes (woonsteden)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "wei-e-frauen-c12",
      "source_id": "wei-e-frauen-s1",
      "quote": "Jacob Grimm noted in particular they might come from Holda, \"Berhta, white by her very name\" and Ostara.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jacob Grimm mencatat mereka mungkin berasal dari Holda, Berhta (\"putih menurut namanya\"), dan Ostara.",
        "en": "Jacob Grimm noted they might come from Holda, Berhta (\"white by her very name\") and Ostara."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Welthund (di Oberbaselbiet disebut Bachpfattli) adalah tokoh legenda berbahasa Jerman: anjing bermata satu di tengah kepala yang gemuk ketika negeri dilanda wabah dan kemiskinan.",
    "en": "The Welthund (called Bachpfattli in the Oberbaselbiet) is a legendary figure of the German-speaking world: a dog with one eye in the middle of its head that grows fat when plague and poverty strike the land.",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Welthund (di Oberbaselbiet disebut Bachpfattli) adalah tokoh legenda (Sagengestalt) dari wilayah berbahasa Jerman. Ia digambarkan sebagai anjing yang bermata satu \"tepat di tengah kepala\". Bila penduduk dan petani hidup baik, ia kurus kering; bila wabah dan kemiskinan melanda, ia justru gemuk. Hampir semua gambaran menyebut ia muncul di lahan gambut, rawa, atau aliran sungai.",
      "en": "The Welthund (called Bachpfattli in the Oberbaselbiet) is a legendary figure (Sagengestalt) of the German-speaking area. It is described as a dog with one eye \"right in the middle of its head\". When the inhabitants and farmers prosper it is thin and lean; when plague and poverty afflict the land it is well fed. Nearly all accounts say it appears near moors, swamps or rivers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "welthund-c01",
        "welthund-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal-usulnya berbeda menurut versi legenda: kadang ia penguasa jahat yang menyiksa petani dan memungut persepuluhan berlebihan, lalu dikutuk Tuhan untuk menjalani sisa hidup sebagai Welthund; kadang ia seorang kolonel atau kapten kapal yang menjual jiwanya kepada Iblis. Pada 2008 film Welthund, berdasarkan novel bersambung Barbara Saladin yang menjalin beberapa legenda Baselbiet, tayang di bioskop Nordwestschweiz.",
      "en": "Its origin differs by version: sometimes it was a wicked lord who tormented farmers and exacted excessive tithes and was condemned by God to live out his life as the Welthund; sometimes a colonel or ship's captain who sold his soul to the Devil.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "welthund-c01",
      "source_id": "welthund-s1",
      "quote": "Der Welthund (im Oberbaselbiet Bachpfattli genannt) ist eine Sagengestalt aus dem deutschen Sprachraum. Er wird als Hund beschrieben, der ein Auge „mitten auf dem Kopf“ trägt. Geht es den Bewohnern und Bauern der Landschaft gut, ist er dünn und mager. Plagen Seuchen und Armut das Land, ist er dagegen gut genährt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Welthund (Bachpfattli di Oberbaselbiet) adalah tokoh legenda (Sagengestalt) wilayah berbahasa Jerman (deutschen Sprachraum): anjing (Hund) bermata satu (Auge) di tengah kepala (Kopf), kurus (mager) saat makmur, gemuk saat wabah (Seuchen) dan kemiskinan (Armut).",
        "en": "The Welthund (Bachpfattli in the Oberbaselbiet) is a legendary figure (Sagengestalt) of the German-speaking area (deutschen Sprachraum): a dog (Hund) with one eye (Auge) mid-head (Kopf), lean (mager) in good times, fat in plague (Seuchen) and poverty (Armut)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "welthund-c02",
      "source_id": "welthund-s1",
      "quote": "Mal war es ein bösartiger Landesherr, der Bauern peinigte und von ihnen übermäßig den Zehnten eintrieb und dafür von Gott dazu verdammt wurde, als Welthund sein weiteres Leben zu fristen, mal war es ein Oberst oder Schiffskapitän, der seine Seele an den Teufel verkaufte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia penguasa jahat (bösartiger Landesherr) yang menyiksa petani (Bauern) dan dikutuk Tuhan (Gott) menjadi Welthund; kadang kolonel (Oberst) atau kapten kapal (Schiffskapitän) yang menjual jiwa (Seele) kepada Iblis (Teufel).",
        "en": "Sometimes a wicked lord (bösartiger Landesherr) who tormented farmers (Bauern), condemned by God (Gott) to be the Welthund; sometimes a colonel (Oberst) or ship's captain (Schiffskapitän) who sold his soul (Seele) to the Devil (Teufel)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "welthund-c03",
      "source_id": "welthund-s1",
      "quote": "Fast allen Schilderungen des Welthunds gemeinsam ist, dass er bei Moorlandschaften, Sümpfen oder Flussläufen auftritt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hampir semua gambaran (Schilderungen) menyebut Welthund muncul di lahan gambut (Moorlandschaften), rawa (Sümpfen), atau aliran sungai (Flussläufen).",
        "en": "Nearly all accounts (Schilderungen) say the Welthund appears near moors (Moorlandschaften), swamps (Sümpfen) or rivers (Flussläufen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "welthund-c04",
      "source_id": "welthund-s1",
      "quote": "2008 lief in den Kulturkinos der Nordwestschweiz der Film Welthund, der auf dem Fortsetzungsroman „Ein etwas anderer Sommer“ von Barbara Saladin basiert.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 2008 film (Film) Welthund, berdasarkan novel bersambung (Fortsetzungsroman) Barbara Saladin, tayang di bioskop Swiss barat laut (Nordwestschweiz).",
        "en": "In 2008 the film (Film) Welthund, based on Barbara Saladin's serialized novel (Fortsetzungsroman), ran in northwestern Swiss (Nordwestschweiz) cinemas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "welthund-c05",
      "source_id": "welthund-s1",
      "quote": "Der Roman verwebt mehrere Baselbieter Sagen zu einer Geschichte, wobei die Sage des Welthunds den Haupthandlungsstrang des Films bildet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Novel (Roman) itu menjalin beberapa legenda Baselbiet (Baselbieter Sagen), dengan legenda Welthund sebagai alur utama (Haupthandlungsstrang) film.",
        "en": "The novel (Roman) weaves several Baselbiet legends (Baselbieter Sagen), with the Welthund legend as the film's main plot (Haupthandlungsstrang)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Witte Wiwer (\"perempuan putih\" dalam bahasa Jerman Rendah) adalah makhluk legenda dari cerita rakyat Jerman (Rendah) yang mirip, tetapi berbeda dari, Weiße Frauen; mereka tinggal di bawah tanah dan bisa menukar bayi dengan anak ganti.",
    "en": "The witte Wiwer (\"white women\" in Low German) are legendary beings of (Low) German folklore similar to but distinct from the weiße Frauen; they live underground and may swap babies for changelings.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "witte-wiwer-c01",
      "witte-wiwer-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Witte Wiwer (juga witte Wîwer, witte Wiewer, atau witte Wiver; semuanya berarti \"perempuan putih\" dalam bahasa Jerman Rendah, Jerman baku weiße Weiber) adalah makhluk legenda dari cerita rakyat Jerman (Rendah) yang mirip tetapi berbeda dari Weiße Frauen. Nama lain mereka adalah unterirdische Weiber (perempuan bawah tanah) di Mecklenburg dan Sibyllen di Jerman barat laut. Artikel tentang Weiße Frauen menyebut witte Wiwer sebagai padanan Jerman Rendah dari Weiße Frauen.",
      "en": "The witte Wiwer (also witte Wîwer, witte Wiewer or witte Wiver; all meaning \"white women\" in Low German, Standard German weiße Weiber) belong to (Low) German folk legend and resemble the weiße Frauen without being the same beings. Other names are unterirdische Weiber (subterranean women) in Mecklenburg and Sibyllen in northwestern Germany. The article on the Weiße Frauen calls the witte Wiwer their Low German equivalent.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "witte-wiwer-c01",
        "witte-wiwer-c02",
        "witte-wiwer-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Witte Wiwer di Mecklenburg tinggal di dalam tanah, di bawah semak, di bukit kecil, di tanah lapang hutan, atau di bawah akar pohon tua. Di Westphalia mereka tinggal di lubang dengan mata air jernih yang dikelilingi hutan kecil, disebut Wittewiwerskule, atau di gua berpintu; mereka juga dikatakan tinggal di pegunungan, dan di pulau Rügen memakai terowongan bawah tanah. Mereka memainkan musik riang dan bersuara sangat merdu.",
      "en": "The witte Wiwer of Mecklenburg make their homes underground: beneath bushes, inside low hills, in woodland glades or among the roots of old trees. In Westphalia they dwell in a pit with a clear spring ringed by a grove, the Wittewiwerskule, or in a cave with a door; they are also said to live in mountains, and on the island of Rügen they use underground tunnels. They play merry music and have very fine voices.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "witte-wiwer-c03",
        "witte-wiwer-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka menukar bayi yang belum dibaptis dengan anak ganti (Wechselbälge) bila tidak ada lampu menyala di samping buaian pada malam hari, dan kadang membawa pergi perempuan yang baru melahirkan. Pada senja hari setiap bulan baru mereka mungkin meminjam kuali pembuat bir, lalu tiga malam kemudian mengembalikannya berisi bir buatan mereka sebagai bayaran. Mereka diburu oleh perburuan liar (wilde Jagd) yang dipimpin pemburu perempuan Fru Waur. Ketika witte Wiwer di Mönchgut di Rügen diusir, pohon ek yang terkait dengan mereka layu, dan mereka meramalkan akan kembali bila pohon itu bertunas lagi.",
      "en": "They swap unbaptized newborns for changelings (Wechselbälge) if no light burns beside the cradle at night, and sometimes carry off women just out of childbed. At twilight each new moon they may borrow the brewing copper and, three evenings later, return it filled with their own beer as payment. They are hunted by the wild hunt (wilde Jagd) led by the huntress Fru Waur. When the witte Wiwer of Mönchgut on Rügen were driven away, the oak tied to them withered, and they foretold their return when it sprouted again.",

        "witte-wiwer-c06",

    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c02",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "are legendary creatures from (Low) German folklore similar to but distinct from the weiße Frauen. Other names are unterirdische Weiber (subterranean women) in Mecklenburg and Sibyllen (sibyls; sg. Sibylle) in Northwestern Germany.",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka makhluk legenda cerita rakyat Jerman (German) yang mirip tetapi berbeda dari weiße Frauen, juga disebut unterirdische Weiber di Mecklenburg dan Sibyllen di Jerman barat laut (Northwestern Germany).",
        "en": "They are legendary creatures of (Low) German folklore akin to but distinct from the weiße Frauen, also called unterirdische Weiber in Mecklenburg and Sibyllen in Northwestern Germany."

    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c03",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "The witte Wiwer of Mecklenburg dwell in the earth, under shrubs, in small hills, in forest clearings, or beneath the roots of old trees.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Witte Wiwer di Mecklenburg tinggal di tanah, di bawah semak, di bukit kecil, di tanah lapang hutan, atau di bawah akar pohon tua.",
        "en": "The witte Wiwer of Mecklenburg dwell in the earth, under shrubs, in small hills, forest clearings or beneath old tree roots."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c04",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "In Westphalia, the witte Wiwer dwell in a pit with a clear fountain inside surrounded by a grove which is called the Wittewiwerskule (white women's pit), otherwise described as a cave with a door. They are also said to dwell in mountains. On the island of Rügen, they further use subterranean tunnels to get from one place to another.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Westphalia mereka tinggal di Wittewiwerskule, lubang bermata air jernih, atau gua berpintu; juga di pegunungan, dan di Rügen memakai terowongan bawah tanah.",
        "en": "In Westphalia they dwell in the Wittewiwerskule, a pit with a clear spring, or a cave with a door; also in mountains, and on Rügen they use subterranean tunnels."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c05",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "The witte Wiwer play merry music and have very fine voices. They exchange unbaptized newborn children for changelings (German Wechselbälge, sg. Wechselbalg) if there is no light burning beside the cradle at night, warding them off.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Witte Wiwer bermusik riang dan bersuara merdu; mereka menukar bayi belum dibaptis dengan anak ganti (Wechselbälge) bila tak ada lampu di samping buaian.",
        "en": "The witte Wiwer play merry music with fine voices; they exchange unbaptized newborns for changelings (Wechselbälge) if no light burns beside the cradle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c06",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "The witte Wiwer might ask to borrow the brewing copper every new moon during twilight hour, taking it away unseen if obliged. Three evenings later, they will return it the very same hour filled with some cans of the beer brewed by them as payment.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Witte Wiwer meminjam kuali bir saat bulan baru, lalu tiga malam kemudian mengembalikannya berisi bir buatan mereka sebagai bayaran.",
        "en": "The witte Wiwer borrow the brewing copper at new moon and three evenings later return it filled with their own beer as payment."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c07",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "They are chased by the wild hunt (German wilde Jagd) from which they flee with flying hair. The wild hunt consists of the wilde Jägerin (wild huntswoman) and her pack of hounds. The wild huntswoman, known as de oll Fru Waur (the old Lady Waur)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka diburu perburuan liar (wild hunt, wilde Jagd) yang dipimpin pemburu perempuan (wilde Jägerin) Fru Waur bersama anjing-anjingnya.",
        "en": "They are chased by the wild hunt (wilde Jagd) of the wild huntswoman (wilde Jägerin) Fru Waur and her hounds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c08",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "They might also take away women just out of childbed and bring them to their dwelling, threatening the woman to stay there or else they would wring her neck.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka kadang membawa perempuan yang baru melahirkan ke tempat tinggal mereka dan mengancamnya.",
        "en": "They may carry women just out of childbed to their dwelling and threaten them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c09",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s1",
      "quote": "When the witte Wiwer of Mönchgut in Rügen were driven away, the oak tree connected to them withered. They prophesied their return when the withered oak would sprout again.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika witte Wiwer di Mönchgut, Rügen, diusir, pohon ek mereka layu; mereka meramalkan kembali bila pohon itu bertunas lagi.",
        "en": "When the witte Wiwer of Mönchgut on Rügen were driven away their oak withered; they prophesied return when it sprouted again."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "witte-wiwer-c10",
      "source_id": "witte-wiwer-s2",
      "quote": "In the Netherlands they are known as the Witte Wieven, and in France as the Dames Blanches. Their Low German equivalent are the Witte Wiwer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Weiße Frauen: padanan Jerman Rendah (Low German) mereka adalah Witte Wiwer; di Belanda (Netherlands) Witte Wieven.",
        "en": "The Weiße Frauen article: their Low German equivalent are the Witte Wiwer; in the Netherlands the Witte Wieven."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Linton Worm adalah makhluk mitos dari legenda Scottish Borders abad ke-12, monster mirip naga yang meneror Linton hingga dibunuh John de Somerville.",
    "en": "The Linton Worm is a mythical beast of a 12th-century Scottish Borders legend, a dragon-like monster that terrorized Linton until slain by John de Somerville.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "worm-of-linton-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Linton Worm adalah makhluk mitos dalam legenda Scottish Borders yang berasal dari abad ke-12; \"Wyrm\" adalah kata Inggris Kuno (Old English) untuk ular. Seorang penulis abad ke-12 menggambarkannya sepanjang tiga Scots yards dan lebih besar dari kaki manusia biasa, berbentuk dan berwarna seperti ular rawa biasa. Mitos ini mirip dengan Lambton Worm.",
      "en": "The Linton Worm is a mythical beast in a Scottish Borders legend dating to the 12th century; \"Wyrm\" is Old English for serpent. A 12th-century writer described it as three Scots yards long and thicker than an ordinary man's leg, shaped and coloured like the common moor adder. The myth resembles that of the Lambton Worm.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "worm-of-linton-c01",
        "worm-of-linton-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Monster itu tinggal di cekungan di sisi timur laut Linton Hill, tempat yang masih disebut \"Worm's Den\", di Linton, Roxburghshire. Ia keluar saat senja dan fajar untuk merusak daerah sekitar, memakan tanaman, ternak, dan manusia, dan kebal terhadap senjata. John de Somerville, Laird of Lariston, mengamati bahwa makhluk itu membuka mulutnya lebar-lebar untuk menelan segalanya, tetapi diam dengan mulut terbuka bila berhadapan dengan sesuatu yang terlalu besar. Ia menyuruh pandai besi menempa tombak berlapis besi yang ujungnya dapat menusuk gumpalan gambut berlumur ter dan belerang, lalu pada fajar menusukkan tombak menyala itu ke mulut dan kerongkongan sang monster hingga terluka parah.",
      "en": "Its home was a hollow on the north-eastern flank of Linton Hill at Linton in Roxburghshire, still called the \"Worm's Den\". It came out at dusk and dawn to ravage the land, eating crops, livestock and people, and no weapon could harm it. John de Somerville, Laird of Lariston, saw that it opened its mouth wide to swallow anything but stayed still, mouth open, before something too large. He had a blacksmith forge an iron-clad spear whose tip could carry a lump of peat dipped in tar and brimstone, and at dawn drove the burning lance into the monster's mouth and throat, mortally wounding it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "worm-of-linton-c03",
        "worm-of-linton-c04",
        "worm-of-linton-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Geliat sekarat Linton Worm konon membentuk bukit-bukit di daerah itu, yang kemudian dikenal sebagai \"wormington\"; ekornya yang mengamuk meruntuhkan gunung di sekelilingnya dan menguburnya selamanya. De Somerville diangkat menjadi pemburu elang kerajaan, dianugerahi gelar kesatria, dan dijadikan \"First Barrone\" Linton; lambang keluarga Somerville adalah wyvern di atas roda. Folklorist Ernest Marwick menyoroti kemiripan cara membunuhnya dengan kisah Stoor worm dari Orkney, yang juga dibunuh dengan gambut menyala. Di atas pintu gereja Linton terdapat \"Somervail Stone\" bergambar kesatria melawan dua binatang, yang dikaitkan dengan kisah ini.",
      "en": "The Linton Worm's death throes supposedly shaped the region's hills, an area that became known as \"wormington\"; its thrashing tail brought the mountain down around it and buried it forever. As a reward De Somerville became Royal Falconer, received a knighthood and the title \"First Barrone\" of Linton; the family's crest was a wyvern on a wheel. The folklorist Ernest Marwick notes the likeness of its slaying to that of the Stoor worm of Orkney, also killed with burning peat. Over the door of Linton church is the \"Somervail Stone\" showing a knight fighting two beasts, linked to this tale.",

        "worm-of-linton-c06",
        "worm-of-linton-c07",
        "worm-of-linton-c08",
        "worm-of-linton-c10"

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ysätters-Kajsa (Ysätter-Kaisa) adalah troll atau penyihir angin dari Närke yang diabadikan Lagerlöf, makhluk usil yang menari di tengah angin puyuh.",
    "en": "Ysätters-Kajsa (Ysätter-Kaisa) is a wind troll or witch of Närke immortalised by Lagerlöf, a mischievous being who dances in the whirlwind.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ysatters-kajsa-c01",
      "ysatters-kajsa-c04",
      "ysatters-kajsa-c11"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ysätters-Kajsa (Ysätter-Kaisa) adalah troll atau penyihir angin dari provinsi Närke yang menurut Lagerlöf tak ada bandingannya di tempat lain. Lagerlöf menampilkannya dalam novel tentang perjalanan Nils Holgersson. Ia dinamai Kaisa karena penyihir angin biasa disebut demikian, dan julukan Ysätter berasal dari rawa Ysätter di paroki Asker; ia tampaknya tinggal di Asker, tetapi bisa muncul di mana saja di Närke. Lagerlöf menutup kisahnya dengan mengatakan bahwa orang kini menganggapnya sudah mati seperti troll lain, tetapi hal itu hampir mustahil dipercaya.",
      "en": "Ysätters-Kajsa (Ysätter-Kaisa) is a wind troll or witch of the province of Närke who, according to Lagerlöf, had no equal elsewhere. Lagerlöf featured her in her novel about the journey of Nils Holgersson. She was called Kaisa because wind witches bear that name, and her byname came from Ysätter swamp in Asker parish; she seemed to live at Asker but could turn up anywhere in Närke. Lagerlöf ends by saying people now think her dead like all other trolls, but that this is almost impossible to believe.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ysatters-kajsa-c01",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c03",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c09",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Lagerlöf, ia bukan troll yang muram melainkan riang dan suka bermain, dan paling senang bila angin badai; ia berdiri di tengah angin puyuh di dataran Närke, rambut panjangnya berputar di antara awan dan roknya menyeret seperti badai debu. Di musim dingin ia menimbulkan badai salju, di musim panas ia mendatangkan hujan saat panen jerami, dan ia meniup tungku arang yang tak dijaga. Namun ia tak jahat sepenuhnya: ia paling keras terhadap orang yang suka bertengkar dan jahat, sering melindungi orang jujur dan anak-anak miskin, dan konon pernah memadamkan kebakaran gereja Asker.",
      "en": "In Lagerlöf's telling she was not a gloomy troll but a merry, playful one who loved a real gale; she stood at the heart of the whirlwinds on the Närke plain, her long hair whirling among the clouds and her skirt trailing like a dust storm. In winter she raised snowstorms, in summer she sent rain on the hay harvest, and she fanned unguarded charcoal kilns. Yet she was not wicked to the bone: she was hardest on the quarrelsome and wicked, often protected honest folk and poor children, and is said once to have put out a fire at the church of Asker.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ysatters-kajsa-c02",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c04",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c06",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c08",
        "ysatters-kajsa-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

    },

      "en": "Dances in the whirlwind.",

    "famous_for": {
      "id": "Usil tetapi melindungi orang jujur.",

      ]
    }
  },
  "did_you_know": {
    "id": "Konon Ysätters-Kajsa pernah memadamkan kebakaran gereja Asker dengan bersarang di tengah asap dan api di atapnya.",
    "en": "Ysätters-Kajsa is said to have once put out a fire at Asker church by nestling among the smoke and flames on its roof.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "abilities": [

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ys%C3%A4tters-Kajsa",
      "title": "Ysätters-Kajsa",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-s2",
      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/10935/pg10935-images.html",
      "title": "The Wonderful Adventures of Nils (terj. Velma Swanston Howard)",
      "author": "Lagerlöf",
      "publisher": "Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "primary-text",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c01",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s2",
      "quote": "In bygone days there was something in Närke the like of which was not to be found elsewhere: it was a witch, named Ysätter-Kaisa.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lagerlöf: dahulu di Närke ada sesuatu yang tak ditemukan di tempat lain, yaitu penyihir (witch) bernama Ysätter-Kaisa.",
        "en": "Lagerlöf: in bygone days Närke had something found nowhere else, a witch named Ysätter-Kaisa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c02",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "Ysätters-Kajsa was not a dark and gloomy troll, but a happy and playful one.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam penggambaran Lagerlöf, Ysätters-Kajsa bukan troll muram melainkan riang dan suka bermain.",
        "en": "In Lagerlöf's portrayal Ysätters-Kajsa was not a gloomy troll but happy and playful."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c03",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s2",
      "quote": "The name Kaisa had been given her because she had a good deal to do with wind and storm--and these wind witches are always so called. The surname was added because she was supposed to have come from Ysätter swamp in Asker parish.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Kaisa diberikan karena ia berurusan dengan angin dan badai (wind and storm), sebutan penyihir angin (wind witches); julukan dari rawa Ysätter (Ysätter swamp) di paroki Asker.",
        "en": "She was named Kaisa because of her dealings with wind and storm, as wind witches are called; the surname came from Ysätter swamp in Asker parish."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c04",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "She would then stand in the centre of the whirlwind. Her long hair would whirl among the clouds, while her skirt would drag on the ground like a dust storm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berdiri di tengah angin puyuh (whirlwind), rambutnya berputar di antara awan (clouds), roknya menyeret seperti badai debu (dust storm).",
        "en": "She stood in the centre of the whirlwind, her hair whirling among the clouds, her skirt dragging like a dust storm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c05",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "Ysätters-Kajsa was not a dark and gloomy troll, but a happy and playful one. What she liked most was a real gale.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ysätters-Kajsa bukan troll muram, melainkan riang dan suka bermain (playful), dan paling menyukai angin badai (gale).",
        "en": "Ysätters-Kajsa was not a gloomy troll but a happy, playful one who most liked a real gale."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c06",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "People noticed that she was hardest on people who were quarrelsome, mean and wicked, but she would often take honest folks and small poor children into her care.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia paling keras terhadap orang yang suka bertengkar dan jahat, tetapi melindungi orang jujur dan anak-anak miskin.",
        "en": "She was hardest on the quarrelsome and wicked, but cared for honest folk and poor children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c07",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "Old people used to say that once, when the church of Asker was burning, Ysätters-Kajsa came, nestled herself among the smoke and fire on the roof of the church, and put it out.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang tua bercerita bahwa ketika gereja Asker terbakar, Ysätters-Kajsa bersarang di asap dan api di atapnya lalu memadamkannya.",
        "en": "Old people said that when the church of Asker burned, Ysätters-Kajsa nestled among the smoke and fire on its roof and put it out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c08",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "Then she would start a real storm and create snow drifts so high that people could hardly get home in the evening.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di musim dingin ia menimbulkan badai (storm) dan timbunan salju (snow drifts) tinggi.",
        "en": "In winter she started a storm and piled snow drifts high."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c09",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s1",
      "quote": "Lagerlöf finishes her presentation of the troll by saying: \"Nowadays, people claim that Ysätters-Kajsa is dead and gone, like all other trolls, but such things are almost impossible to believe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lagerlöf menutup penggambarannya: kini orang berkata Ysätters-Kajsa sudah mati seperti troll lain, tetapi hal itu hampir mustahil dipercaya.",
        "en": "Lagerlöf ends her portrait: people now claim Ysätters-Kajsa is dead like all other trolls, but that is almost impossible to believe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c10",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s2",
      "quote": "Old people say of her that, once, when Asker church was burning, Ysätter-Kaisa swept through the air, lit amid fire and smoke on the church roof, and averted the disaster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lagerlöf: orang tua bercerita ketika gereja Asker (Asker church) terbakar, Ysätter-Kaisa hinggap di atap di tengah api dan asap dan mencegah bencana.",
        "en": "Lagerlöf: old people say that when Asker church burned, Ysätter-Kaisa lit on the roof amid fire and smoke and averted disaster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ysatters-kajsa-c11",
      "source_id": "ysatters-kajsa-s2",
      "quote": "He who fancies that Ysätter-Kaisa is dead and gone may as well hear what occurred in Närke the year that Nils Holgersson travelled over that part of the country.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lagerlöf mengaitkan Ysätter-Kaisa dengan kisah tahun ketika Nils Holgersson melintasi Närke.",
        "en": "Lagerlöf ties Ysätter-Kaisa to what happened in Närke the year Nils Holgersson travelled over it."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ziphius adalah hewan dongeng (fabeldier): burung hantu air sebesar paus dengan paruh berbentuk V dan mulut sangat besar yang menyerang setiap kapal.",
    "en": "The ziphius is a fabulous beast (fabeldier): a water owl as big as a whale with a V-shaped beak and an enormous mouth that attacks every ship.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ziphius adalah hewan dongeng (fabeldier). Ia adalah burung hantu air (wateruil) sebesar paus (walvis), berparuh berbentuk V dan bermulut sangat besar, dan menyerang setiap kapal yang mendekat. Nama ziphius kemudian dipakai untuk paus paruh Cuvier (Ziphius cavirostris).",
      "en": "The ziphius is a fabulous beast (fabeldier). It is a water owl (wateruil) as big as a whale (walvis), with a V-shaped beak and an enormous mouth, and it attacks every ship that comes near. The name ziphius was later given to Cuvier's beaked whale (Ziphius cavirostris).",
